# Feature List

- FDTD preparation warns about incident-line Yee band-edge and waveform DC content using each line's discrete transverse wavenumbers and drive clock, including integer-step fixed-k drives; real-line diagnostics remain bitwise unchanged, and declared waveforms and compiled source terms are unchanged.
- Structures that reach a PML interface are continued through the PML along the face normal; non-prismatic contacts, mesh geometry and surface-impedance conductors warn instead of extruding.
- Differentiable FDTD module scenes keep numerical material declarations for result monitors and postprocessing; graph-enabled declarations are retained only by mode launch routing and replay. Field objectives retain their adjoint gradients.
- Core FDTD step graphs omit redundant PEC clamps using graph-build checks of compiled source patch destinations and electric dispersion. Existing core E face-zero stores supply enforcement when no later writer can touch the faces; face sources, separate physics and complex-field enforcement retain conservative clamps. TFSF clamp scheduling is preserved.
- Gradients through ModeSource/ModePort launch-plane material use matching differentiable dense vector profiles in forward and replay, for isolated lossless modes with operator dimension n ≤ 4096. Lossy or conductive trainable planes, complex selected eigenvalues, degenerate modes and larger trainable planes raise explicitly. Both vector launch paths share scale-relative Yee power normalization and float64-relative E/H component pruning. Replay retains both mode-profile and electric injection-coefficient derivatives through the no-grad time-stepping backward. The forward material graph and exact launch-plane slices are retained for preparation and backward replay without another material compile; routing and gauge state stay outside resume fingerprints, and a source's phase-reference index stays fixed across forwards of one Scene/SceneModule while its magnitude remains at least half the peak.
- Modal unit-power normalization and modal power fractions use cancellation bounds relative to the same transverse field products and area quadrature, admitting micrometre apertures without an absolute watt floor. Positive incident-power ratios, source-spectrum normalization, finite source patches and RF reference-impedance checks likewise respect their physical scale. Identically zero source spectra are refused. CUDA integration includes preparation of every shipped example without time stepping or optimization.
- Analytic source vectors and mode-surface profiles suppress roundoff components at or below `64 * eps(float64)` of each owning vector or same-unit profile family, preserving weak physical components and axis-aligned term counts at metre and micrometre scale. Custom current and field datasets skip only exactly zero sampled patches; modal-drive vectors retain their own dtype-relative guards.
- FDTD backward rejects trainable material on a ModeSource or ModePort launch plane when forward and replay mode profiles use different solver families; fixed launch planes and matching scalar mode solves retain their existing behavior. Plane-dependence verdicts are cached per forward material graph and input tensor version, and source replay preserves forward launch metadata and resume identity.
- Core-path validation supports opt-in Rule-2 G1 two-horizon run comparisons and G4 setup comparisons through the shared numerical-equivalence comparator, with per-tensor T0/T1/T2 receipts and exact discrete leaves. Bitwise remains the default. A CPU determinism command checks every saved tensor exactly across at least three independent capture directories.
- MaterialRegion and geometry smoothing is scale-invariant with `witwin>=0.4.6`: the cell-relative smoothing width is no longer clamped to an absolute float32-epsilon length, so micrometre-scale design regions no longer smear over about three cells (metre-scale results are unchanged).
- Native CUDA FDTD source patches use prepared, ordered cell gathers for overlapping TFSF faces, periodic and Bloch images, batched sources, and core-path periodic injection. Each target has one writer and a fixed contribution order, so repeated GPU runs are bitwise identical; historical bit-identity baselines are unchanged.
- Periodic node material-normal reconstruction uses one shared nonuniform central derivative at duplicate endpoint planes, preserving reciprocal full-tensor Yee couplings under subpixel averaging. Interior reconstruction, edge-window diagonals and arithmetic averaging retain their existing calculations.

- `Simulation.fdtd(..., fdtd_core_path="auto", accelerated_setup="auto")` defaults eligible initializing scenes to the CUDA core and independently dispatched accelerated setup. Admission blockers, resume/non-initializing preparation and material-cache overrides retain existing stepping; failed core activation or whole-step capture retries with a fresh existing-path solver. Results and solver statistics report `execution_path` and `fallback_reason`; public `FDTDCoreFallbackWarning` warns once per Simulation on automatic fallback. Explicit `True` preserves fail-closed core admission and explicit `False` preserves existing stepping without warnings. PMC faces, including PMC symmetry planes, and closed-transverse CPML (refused before preparation for an explicit cpml/stablepml absorber) retain existing stepping.
- `accelerated_setup=True` enables independent CUDA material, coefficient and mesh-distance pieces while preserving normal stepping. Unsupported pieces retain PyTorch, including in port, copper surface, thin-sheet, wire, closed-surface-monitor and dispersive scenes. `fdtd_core_path=True` still enables setup and retains its fail-closed stepping admission.
- Point spectral observer setup batches exact nearest-node queries per Yee component with bounded float64 NumPy temporaries and caches declaration normalization by value. Result assembly shares immutable metadata and retains the existing group layouts, spectral builder, scaling arithmetic, frequency-selection splits and monitor order. `python -m benchmark.core_path.f1b_observer_timing` compares CPU compilation, preparation and export for the original 16,384-point F1 sample without stepping.
- Accelerated bulk material setup admits fixed float64 Box/Sphere/Cylinder/Pyramid/PolySlab parameters and boolean trees using the reference query precision and dimension-conversion order. Mesh declarations, conductor acceleration and binary pole ownership retain their float32 scope. Compiled tensors are bitwise equal to the PyTorch compile on GPU for the float64 primitive cases and the original F1 datagen samples; `benchmark.core_path.setup_gates g4` accepts `float64_*` primitive cases and original F1 datagen catalog names, with per-tensor T0/T1/T2 JSON and Markdown tables under `--equivalence rule2`.
- S0/S1 draft extends setup with Cylinder distance/coverage, Pyramid triangle distance, PolySlab polygon distance, PEC/PMC distance sampling and conformal PEC edge fill, chronological lossy Kottke sample accumulation, and native primitive coverage for periodic images. Occupancy tanh, periodic union/seam arithmetic and slab taper remain PyTorch; compiled tensors are bitwise equal to the PyTorch compile on GPU.
- Accelerated setup includes dispersive node-weight accumulation and fixed fp32 Box/Sphere/Cylinder binary pole edges under `kottke_edge`, with declaration-rounded closed box bounds and carrier fill support. Graded/trainable uniform pole fields, trainable geometry, modulation, perturbations, material regions, full tensor backgrounds and periodic/Bloch pole edges retain PyTorch. Compiled tensors are bitwise equal to the PyTorch compile on GPU, including the dispersive setup oracle.
- With the forward-accurate default `SubpixelSpec(averaging="kottke_edge")`, piecewise-constant constituents with scalar or spatially uniform custom Drude, Lorentz and Debye poles use surface-inclusive binary constituent ownership at each Yee component centre in FDTD and FDFD. Ownership uses float64 scene axes and closed-box bound comparisons. A float32-stored box face snaps to the nearest grid plane within the propagated declaration-rounding bound for `position +/- size/2`: half a position ULP, one quarter of a size ULP, the division's rounding residual, and half a face ULP, compared in float64. ULP uses the larger adjacent representable spacing; float64 bounds compare exactly, with no ownership epsilon. Pole scope follows actual parameter variation, decided by exact equality of every per-cell strength value with no tolerance or small-strength threshold (a field differing by one ulp stays graded; a 1e-30 strength is an ordinary pole): uniform custom fields (including custom Sellmeier and zero pole-strength perturbations) compile identically to their scalar equivalents; genuinely varying fields retain previous fractional weights and backgrounds in every mode, including kottke_edge. Any sampling window touching such a graded constituent retains fractional weights for all constituents, including a piecewise-constant neighbour. A binary scalar window takes the centre constituent's complete pole set and instantaneous permittivity/permeability, including the nondispersive side; dielectric-only windows retain their selected subpixel averaging. Explicit arithmetic (including samples=1), polarized, polarized_tensor and kottke_smooth modes retain fractional pole weights. Dispersive density optimization uses arithmetic or polarized gradient modes and carries the fractional-weight interface error; the default kottke_edge rejects trainable density. FDTD differentiates bulk ADE scalar pole parameters and direct custom oscillator-strength grids through native PyTorch material-value workflows on fixed geometry. Pole-parameter gradients through split-trace conductor histories fail closed. Existing FDFD adjoint capability limits remain. Surface-conductivity poles keep their surface discretization.
- Sub-cell negative-permittivity metal interfaces remain resolution-limited: metals require a skin-depth-resolving grid or a surface-impedance/conformal treatment. Binary pole assignment removes fractional interface ADE weights; it does not resolve an under-sampled skin depth.
- FDTD phasors use physical Yee labels: post-step E at `(n+1)dt` and H at `(n+1/2)dt`, with the existing magnetic half-step stagger. Source references retain provider compensation and select their Fourier label from the compiled provider and waveform. Compared with earlier results, raw ordinary field/port phasors rotate by `exp(i omega dt)`; normalized additive-current/direct-drive phasors rotate by `exp(i omega dt/2)`, uncompensated magnetic/sheet/analytic-pulse phasors by `exp(i omega dt)`, and retained auxiliary/CW references stay unchanged to rounding. Same-frequency S/Z/Gamma, flux, matched field/port powers and directivity remain unchanged. Raw wire clocks retain their physical labels. Field-time trace `t` advances by one dt; values are unchanged and E/H offsets are 0/-1/2. Format-6 resume checkpoints record the origin and provider reference; earlier spectral conventions require a fresh run.

- O3b draft extends core setup with native Kottke window reductions, polarized reconstruction, fused update coefficients and hash-built coefficient tables, bitwise equal to the PyTorch compile on GPU.
- `Simulation.fdtd(absorber="auto")` is the default: real-field closed-transverse PML selects graded sigma with >= 40 explicit layers; open and complex scenes retain CPML. Thin automatic graded layers fail closed with the effective transverse axes, deficient face layer counts, and both remedies (explicit guarded CPML or >= 40 layers); distributed automatic graded selection also fails closed. Results record `absorber` and `absorber_resolution_reason`; explicit absorber profiles remain available.
- Main single-device CPML/stable-PML runs in real closed-transverse or complex nonzero fixed-k topologies have a checkpointed energy-growth guard. It fits 16 maxima windows of 2,048 steps, sampled every 32 steps. When slope + 3 standard errors exceeds 4e-7/step, the envelope rises, and energy exceeds 1e-10 of peak, real fields fail closed with `FDTDPassivityError`; complex fixed-k runs warn once with `RuntimeWarning` and continue (user decision D2). `Result.metadata["passivity_guard"]` reports `warned: complex fixed-k growth`, and `passivity_guard_warning` records slope, stderr, window start/end steps and warning step; solver statistics carry the same fields. A warned state survives checkpoint/resume without re-warning (schema 6 retained). Measured fixed-k rates at resolution 160: 2.1e-7/step for the point-dipole eps4 slab; 3.5–5.0e-7/step for eps4 TM CW at 1.1 f0 and at 20 degrees; 7.0–7.5e-7/step for the vacuum TM CW arm and 8.2e-7/step for the eps4 TM slab CW, both at 0.25 k0, 0.9 f0; and up to 7.7e-6/step (vacuum TM CW at 0.55 k0) and 8.2e-6/step (vacuum TM CW at 20 degrees with 600 transient cycles), with 7.7e-6 ± 1.9e-6/step in an early window of a 320,000-step pulsed record. Growth slower than 4e-7 per step (about 1.5x energy over 1e6 steps) is not detected. CopperFoil modal storage (both complex banks) and two-sided electric trace masses are counted; thin sheets use the qualified modified Newmark energy, including split E/H, chord fields, recovered sheet flux and copper modal storage, with device-only float64 sampling. Mixed CopperFoil/thin-sheet scenes include the split-trace magnetic cross term in the modified energy and keep native and sheet energy widths separate; lossless and lossy 300-step closed CPU cavity controls close energy plus copper heat at 1e-13 relative. CPU energy/heat balance, qualified-functional equivalence and active copper/sheet guard controls pass. Source settling/transient gating and workarounds are documented in `docs/reference/fdtd-cpml.md`; the core path blocks closed-transverse CPML pending guard support. GPU qualification is complete. Scenes with uncounted storage (wires, networks, coax, SIBC, dispersive or gyromagnetic media) are not guarded; in v0.6.0 scenes with CopperFoil or thin sheets are also skipped (their counted energy costs 39-133% of a step to sample every 32 steps; see `docs/reference/fdtd-cpml.md`); `Result.metadata["passivity_guard"]` reports the status.


- Eligible `fdtd_core_path=True` scenes also use exact GPU material setup with native sphere, rotated box and boolean sampling, ordered material blends and sample accumulation; other material compiles retain the PyTorch path.
- O3c draft adds scoped CUDA fp32 fixed-mesh distance evaluation with a cached device LBVH and supplied static-tree support, including mesh/box boolean setup. Winding, smooth sign and mesh coverage retain torch arithmetic; mesh equality gates and 128³/256³ setup timings await GPU qualification.

- Eligible single-GPU float32 forward scenes use one fused magnetic and one fused electric Yee launch per step, with exact coefficient tables and dense or slab CPML. Point, plane, flux, finite-plane and closed-surface monitors are supported. Explicit `fdtd_core_path=True` raises for unsupported configurations; `"auto"` records their first blocker and retains existing execution.
- Eligible axis-aligned auxiliary-line TFSF core graphs combine each field correction with its opposite auxiliary-line update in one launch, using the full prepared absorber profiles. Two-cell incident-boundary continuation uses one separately rounded CUDA launch; its ghost-cell dependencies prevent folding into the magnetic grid. Oblique providers and wider ghost stencils retain their existing paths. Results are bitwise equal to the existing path on A6000 (sm_86); the 128³ TFSF row runs 11.5% faster and the 256³ row 1.9% faster.
- The core path gathers grouped point/plane spectra in one launch per 16 groups, with prepared storage validation and explicitly rounded float32/float64 fused accumulation. Bit-identity gate passed (A6000); step time measured about 3% faster at 128³ (slab 0.2976 to 0.2889 ms, no-PML 0.2429 to 0.2345 ms), no regression elsewhere.
- The core path uses 32-j-block strips when the Yee plane reuse footprint exceeds two thirds of device L2, recording electric and magnetic launch decisions in the prepared runtime. The factor is calibrated only on A6000 (sm_86, 6 MiB L2); sm_89/sm_90/sm_100 remain unvalidated and each needs a slab spot check. Bit-identity and timing gates passed (A6000).
- The core path reads live electric coefficients for Kerr, second-order nonlinear response, two-photon absorption, time modulation (including instantaneous nonlinear composition), and deterministic dielectric breakdown. Modulation retains its FP64 device clock and reconstructs resume endpoints from the saved int64 step counter alone; coefficient and breakdown updates keep their ordered launches in whole-step graphs. Modulation with separate physics retains per-step execution.
- W3 core correctness gates include disjoint modulation frequencies with unmodulated edges, graded-kappa dense/slab CPML, PEC without CPML, and same-material modulation with Kerr or two-photon absorption. Whole-step capture and bitwise comparisons remain GPU acceptance requirements.
- W1 admits lumped/coaxial/terminal ports and elements, wave-port modal sources/monitors, CopperFoil, conformal two-sided thin sheets and sheet feeds, thin wires, PEC cut surfaces, SIBC, circuits and embedded networks to the fused Yee pair. W2 captures ordered whole steps for copper, thin sheets (including feeds), ports, lossless/lossy wires and native embedded networks, with capture-state restoration. Circuits, circuit-bound ports, SIBC, cut surfaces, host-driven wire monitors, and separate physics combined with TFSF or magnetic sources retain the per-step loop. Fields, port, copper, sheet and wire outputs are bitwise equal to the existing path at chunk sizes 32, 16 and 1.
- W6 phase 1 admits electric Debye, Drude, Lorentz, mixed/multiple poles, Sellmeier, custom poles, conductive dispersive media and graphene ADE sheets to the core path, including whole-step lumped-port capture. Magnetic ADE uses the same ordered helpers when the independent permeability and magnetic-conductivity guards permit it. Existing ADE state and updates are preserved; explicit-True dispersive whole-step capture failures restore state and raise, while automatic requests rebuild existing execution. GPU qualification is complete: the W6 G1 scenes, including whole-step capture, are bitwise equal to the existing path.

- Eligible core forward runs prepare exact device-indexed source and DFT rows and capture whole steps in chunks of 32 (internal validation also selects 1 or 16). Small point-monitor spectra retain native point accumulation inside both ordinary and separate-physics graphs using exact device-indexed weight rows. Chunk boundaries preserve shutoff, progress, plotting and stop steps. Automatic capture failures rebuild existing execution; explicit-True requests retain restored core stepping for nondispersive scenes and raise for dispersive scenes.
- Core admission includes modal, custom-field, custom electric/magnetic current, uniform-current and astigmatic-beam sources, plus modal, diffraction and permittivity monitors. Field/flux time monitors retain the per-step observer tail with the fused Yee pair. Their fields and monitor outputs are bitwise equal to the existing path; PMC boundaries remain refused.

- `port_voltage` and `port_phasors` project caller-supplied Yee phasors through prepared lumped, coaxial and unbound terminal geometry with native FDTD probe/branch semantics and field gradients. `dt=None` selects continuous time. `Result.port_drive` exposes the prescribed spectrum and resolved source resistance with the run's per-bin normalization; `trapezoidal_impedance` is exported at package level. Wire, circuit, network, modal and sheet-gap hooks reject explicitly.

- Native FDTD port metadata declares `coupled` (midpoint voltage and branch current) or `probe` (instantaneous E-line voltage and Ampere H-loop current). Mixing these voltages carries `cos(omega dt/2)`. A probe leaves PEC intact; preparation rejects a fully PEC-covered probe gap.

- External reference exports preserve oblique TFSF physical incident power and use the runtime's resolved Yee coordinates for snapped spectral and time-domain power planes, including modal and diffraction planes. Benchmark keys invalidate earlier references only when the exported amplitude factor or plane coordinate changes; identical exports retain their existing keys.
- CW benchmark references use a longer observation horizon to suppress finite-history phasor bias: at least 1000 vacuum domain-diagonal crossing times plus the source ramp offset. Pulse-only export durations stay unchanged; cache keys record the extended duration only when it changes the export. Direct exports can request this rule with `cw_steady_state=True`.
- Integration `plan` and `run` support `--in-place` on uploaded source trees without git, verify the commit label against upload metadata, and accept `--changed-files` for component selection. Worktrees provision reference caches from the main checkout while preserving tracked cache files. Plans and merged reports record cache hashes, entry counts and bytes, rejecting shard cache mismatches. Baseline environment settings reach child processes; required BITID baseline skips fail execution and merged reports. In-place units referencing missing or excluded staged docs are recorded separately as INFRA, without pass/fail counts or a phase-1 stop; reports flag phase 1 as incomplete when affected. Manifest `docs_text_mentions` entries exempt exact extracted paths used only as text.
- Integration runs accept explicit unit lists, string shard labels, atomic per-unit claims after lane locking, and an optional free-memory threshold. Benchmark scenarios shard independently into fresh interpreters with separate result artifacts; reports aggregate the benchmark suite and list failing scenarios. Reports collect only shard plans and sort units by ID, including INFRA units. Planning checks reference keys on CPU and lists missing or stale caches as INFRA units that skip execution; the explicit trust-cache setting skips key comparison.
- Numerical-equivalence test helpers return per-tensor metric rows for setup, step and two-horizon run bounds, with exact discrete comparisons and at most 4× horizon growth. Optional path-to-family mappings or callables (None means no family) bound step/run structural-zero components below 1e-6 of their reference family's maximum against that threshold, flagging those rows and recording `family` and `family_max_ref`; setup rejects families and unmapped tensors retain per-tensor bounds. Full-horizon structural-zero differences within their floored bound pass the growth check even above 4×; both horizons must still meet their bounds. Batched device reductions support 85,000-leaf trees with one metric transfer per device/dtype group. Opt-in pinned-baseline receipts feed T0/T1/T2 batch tables and mover lists in Markdown and JSON. Plans require T1 movers to be listed with measured relative differences and T2 movers with approval references; intended and discrete movers always require T2.

- (Qualified, not sampled by default in v0.6.0.) Sheet guard sampling recovers the full-row minimum-norm flux with a prepared sparse range factorization, two triangular solves and one sparse matvec, including inconsistent forcing and closed loops. The sparse CSR triangular solves run through torch on the current stream (cuSPARSE on CUDA), deterministic and without per-sample host reads. New CUDA flux matvecs use fixed row gathers and sums without floating-point atomic accumulation. Validated host material coefficients and circulation operators are reused for diagnostic setup. Guard decisions and the qualified energy functional are unchanged. The bounded cost script has a separate prepare timeout (1200 s default). See `docs/reference/fdtd-cpml.md`.

- Guard storage qualification inventories deduplicated tensor bytes on CPU, including copper/sheet/mixed controls and PCB grid totals, with bounded reviewer-owned CUDA allocation and graph-capture checks. Sheet energy caches initialize on first sampling request, so guard-off stepping retains zero sampling bytes. A separate bounded guard-on/off cost script logs every phase, enforces watchdogs and a memory cap, and saves results after each scene. Shipped state in 0.6.0: closed-transverse CPML scenes with CopperFoil or thin sheets report the guard as skipped (energy storage not counted) at zero sampling cost, and the counted copper/sheet functional ships disabled. Its measured 32-step sampling cost (39-133% of a step) is the 0.6.1 item. See `docs/reference/fdtd-cpml.md`.
- Integration manifest parse errors identify the file, line, column and nearest suite, known-failure, expected-mover or merge-declaration table, including its branch, commit or ID when present. Structural validation rejects missing required root keys and unknown keys in these array tables, identifying declarations that accidentally capture root settings. CPU self-tests guard the real manifest and reproduce misplaced header declarations.
- Integration batches support commit-bound planning, balanced file shards, fresh interpreter execution with optional per-unit locks, merged JUnit/JSON failure reports and isolated git bisection. A data-only component map scopes pytest by `--since`; repeatable `--exclude-component` omits full component suites with explicit operator-exclusion reporting while retaining all always-on suites, with unique test ownership and per-component GPU-hour estimates. Integration-tier component units exclude milestone suite files/nodes and the plan lists them as milestone-only; `--tier milestone` retains them with overlaps executed once. Collection errors stop planning; BITID reports retain assertion messages and captured JUnit output tails. Baseline-first execution supports policy overrides, UTC progress and lock patterns. Exclusion windows defer new units using a 1.5x estimate margin, including lock waits. Manifest annotations separate known and expected failures; known-only phase-1 failures continue. Explicit `--two-gpu` admission enables the Batch-1 slot; `--tier milestone` additionally admits the full declared two-device suite. Other two-device cases remain NOT RUN, and exception-contract movers require matching failure text.
- Multi-frequency antenna postprocessing reuses closed-surface payloads, with fresh nested containers on every public read, and validates frequency-independent exterior media once. Pointwise spectral currents share validated area weights; far-field transforms share quadrature geometry and one angular batch's direction projection while retaining separate per-frequency current integrals and computing only the electric field needed by antenna metrics. The developer comparison script supports a selected baseline revision and host-side cProfile output. The first optimization passed every GPU bit-identity comparison (printed-dipole first call 39.7 to 9.4 s; half-wave dipole 12.1 to 3.5 s); follow-up GPU identity and timing verification remain pending.
- FDTD honours explicit `full_field_dft=False` for multi-frequency runs: volume-field access raises with the opt-in fix, and automatic electric PowerLossMonitor channels fail when `Result.power_loss` evaluates them without explicit electric fields; `None` retains automatic volume recording. Ensemble and port-sweep placement estimates use the same resolved volume-DFT request as execution.

- FDFD `PointDipole` profiles now have unit Yee control-volume integral, matching FDTD's physical current moment in A m. CW results use peak phasors with `exp(-i omega t)` and preserve the declared amplitude and phase. This replaces the legacy Gaussian-reference mass and ideal-profile calibration; existing FDFD dipole field amplitudes change.

- `Scene.to_dict()` and `Scene.from_dict()` round-trip fixed declarative scenes as JSON-safe `witwin.scene/1` dictionaries, with explicit constructor defaults, shared material identities, core and sheet geometries, sources, ports, loads, monitors, and optional large-array references. An absent background remains `{"material": null}`, which dictionary consumers interpret as vacuum. Unsupported scene capabilities raise `TypeError`.
  `ModeABC` (`mode_abc` faces) is serialized.

- Scalar pFFT PMCHWT lifting uses body-local shared-grid spectra of stable medium differences, analytic incident traces and divergences, and exact-minus-grid near-face precorrection for single and batched RHS builds, including weak contrasts; RHS receipts record the selected grid and near-pair counts.

- Leaf principal blocks and LU setup are batched by size, retaining the original operator action, identities and receipt schema. The acceptance preparation profiler reports surface compilation, projection, grid kernels, exterior/interior precorrection, leaf setup and lifted RHS stages without a solve; GPU timing and numerical equivalence validation remain pending.

- Random-scene label qualification uses F1 base resolution `ceil(max(20, 10*n_max) * wavelengths)`, records `ppw` and `n_max`, and selects `kottke_edge` averaging (four samples per axis) or `polarized` (three). Deterministic cost-balanced shards include printed/wire per-step overhead in GPU-hour estimates, write isolated `shard-<i>` outputs, and combine through `--report --shard-count N` before convergence analysis. The default averaging requires the edge-averaging compiler update.

- The FDFD PML now absorbs instead of reflecting. It is the frequency-domain form of the FDTD CPML, so one `BoundarySpec.pml(...)` means the same absorber in both solvers. The stretch `s = 1 + i sigma/(omega eps0)` uses a cubic `sigma` graded by physical depth, with `sigma_max = -4 ln(1e-6)/(2 eta0 d)`, and is sampled at each Yee position. `strength` in (0, 100] scales `sigma_max`; 0 or a value above 100 (including the 1e6 default) keeps it nominal. The former index-graded `1 + i*strength*u^2` stretch reflected at the default strength. Compared with Mie on a dielectric sphere at 16 cells per wavelength, the extinction error falls from 66% to 4.7% and the far-field error from 7.8% to 1.8%. A one-edge gap now matches the Hertzian radiation resistance within 0.5%, where it previously gave 0.1% of it. Results no longer depend on PML thickness (8 against 15 layers: 4.5e-5) or on strength, and the complex128 system stays symmetric to 3e-16. Every FDFD result with a PML changes, and small strengths now act as multipliers of `sigma_max` rather than setting the absolute stretch.

- Dense CPML electric and magnetic kernels retain the declared scalar type through derivative, convolution-memory and curl intermediates. Actual six-component active/inactive controls satisfy the unchanged FP64 update gate, while all72 measured float32 outputs remain bitwise equal. The existing bounded public FP64 forward/persistence/material-sensitivity contracts pass; precision admission and unsupported execution/material guards are unchanged.

- Distributed CPML replay checks retain the original all-face automatic memory path and add a controlled x-only sensitivity case. Actual nonzero native checkpoints demonstrate that swapped auxiliary keys and dropped history fail the unchanged comparators. Twenty GPU fixture controls pass without skips; original failures remain recorded.

- Independent whole-body bent-board refinement reproduces a fixed uniform1.25mm reference within0.532% complex field and0.679% power, with physical flux error below1e-6. Actual sources and compiled body operators are verified; finer-grid continuum convergence remains pending.

- Distributed FDTD result statistics support shard coordinators without reading or gathering a global Ex tensor. Actual local precision and validated coordinator precision retain existing metadata semantics. Fifty-one CPU contracts and two real two-GPU gradient controls pass; final-source spectral/time regression also passes39 actual GPU cases.

- Magnetic CPML grading samples actual Yee half points on both boundary sides, including nonuniform grids. Matched copper controls reduce the asymmetric right return from 7.36%/2.93% to about 1.12e-5/1.08e-6 for 12/24 layers; thirteen Cartesian, mixed-boundary and gradient GPU controls pass. Residual boundary reflection and full PCB convergence remain separate.

- Printed-line diagnostics can retain a fixed physical primary V/I contour and separately named mesh-native secondary contour. Forty-four CPU controls and four actual 1,024-step forwards prove unchanged Norton stamps, physical fields and primary samples, including Graph and checkpoint resume. Secondary public observations reconstruct exactly; full attenuation and mesh convergence remain separate.
- Printed-line accuracy has an independent CPU 2D cross-section reference (Yee full-wave eigen solver with complex permittivity, finite-volume quasi-static solver, surface-impedance Cu loss on 35 um foils) with Richardson-refined beta/alpha/Z for the public CPW and coupled pair. FDTD beta and total alpha pass 1%/5%; separated Cu alpha is 40-60% low and the fixed 2.5 mm V/I functional is not a Z estimator. Public-line columns and reducers accept explicit dx/padding/sigma convergence overrides.
- Printed-line Cu V/I validation has an explicit single-column CPW/coupled-pair workflow through public `Result.query_fields`. It retains signed physical contours, requires complete producer clocks and independently checks native Point primitives plus both constitutive sides through real checkpoint and Result persistence. Default stripline pilots and mode/attenuation accuracy gates remain separate.
- Public CPW/coupled-pair line columns now capture their executed Cu-loss realization (B/rates/D, native support, sampling normalization, records and source spectra), hash-bound to the executed copper-operator signature. A strict CPU consumer validates provenance, clock, film-work replay and tail fields before the original seven basis gates; a separate hash-gated receipt recovers the completed first CPW column without rerunning fields.

- Full-regression launch requires separate 16 GiB reserves on temporary and output filesystems and redirects new temporary data to the large artifact volume. Sixteen CPU controls include real child interruption on either reserve breach; all 536 retained original-input hashes remain unchanged after the core upgrade.

- Consumer metadata preserves original provider hashes and unqualified status when binding unchanged closed cases to a new consumer. Explicit helper import/call closure supplies retained matrices only to consuming test modules and rejects missing or conflicting originals and changed helper sources. Seventeen maintained CPU controls pass; final source and native execution remain separate.

- Cartesian native spectral plane power forms paired Yee E/H products before physical cell projection. Actual copper side traces, sided exterior endpoints and exact source-normalization metadata preserve Torch gradients and coherent-drive cross terms. Common-point and mapped presentations identify their separate functional. The same-source spectral/time/legacy matrix passes 39 actual GPU controls; complete PCB convergence remains separate.

- Real Cartesian time-flux monitors use native paired products and actual positive-side copper Et with persistent GPU sampling buffers. Output metadata declares E(t)/H(t-dt/2); time outputs remain nondifferentiable. Unsupported mapped, magnetic and ambiguous-sided contexts reject explicitly. Native ABI4 prevents reuse of old packaged time-flux signatures.

- Seven complete 12 ns, 41-frequency printed-dipole mesh/padding/material controls pass their declared physical gates. Independent public reloads verify full compressed/raw hashes and reproduce accepted V/I power, flux and Cu/bulk heat. The eight fine/finer controls and full antenna convergence remain pending.

- Complete maintained regression binds original artifact inputs separately for each test module during collection and execution. Real CPU lifecycle/isolation controls and a complete 8,325-node collection pass; failures, skips, timeouts and historical source identities remain explicit. Final release execution uses a separately frozen source and native ABI.

- Package metadata requires `witwin>=0.4.2,<0.5` so installation selects the shared-core boolean geometry (`Union`, `Intersection`, `Difference`) and `Mesh.from_stl` in addition to the 0.4.1 geometry ancestry and fixed assignment-control fixes. Environment installation and final integrated regression remain separate steps.

- Bounded stripline two-drive diagnostics preserve actual source spectra, native copper-side states and shared bulk/film/flux inference across Graph and disk resume. Seventy-one CPU and four actual real-source GPU controls pass. Gaussian carrier-phase semantics and weak-component error limits are explicit; full-duration propagation and two-length attenuation remain pending.

- Independent bent-board observer isolation now passes all twelve actual closed-surface, 72/96 ns and FD8/16 controls at the fixed 5 mm grid. Unfitted complex-field/power changes stay below 0.053%/0.059%; the separate 10.004% fine-mesh radiation increment remains unresolved.

- Independent printed-line reference preparation audits actual coherent source signs, passive gaps, fixed exterior grids and bounded output estimates, with twelve negative controls. The balanced stripline short-run magnetic parity control passes; full propagation, loss and matching native fine-grid tests remain pending.

- Refined bent-PCB controls preserve physical PEC/source support through the completed 1.25 mm independent reference. The last radiation-power mesh increment remains 10.004%, so radiation convergence is still unqualified.

- Printed-dipole substrate refinement can explicitly preserve exterior vacuum nodes and all six radiation-face interpolation supports. Thirty CPU contracts validate fixed physical geometry, terminal moments and three combined grids; exact storage preflight exposes the finest 41-frequency full-field DFT exceeding 48 GiB, so physical execution requires bounded observations.

- Conductive-port and copper VJP checks now use independent gradient-disabled ordinary forwards for field, loss, stored energy, accepted power and reflection. All 37 controls pass, including phase-sensitive complex reflection in direct and MNA feeds, with the original gradient thresholds preserved.

- Pulsed FDTD spectra use an executed-record Fourier integral per reference period that preserves amplitude units and removes planned-horizon scaling from fields, ports, flux, radiation and spectral losses. `Simulation.fdtd(..., reference_frequency=None)` defaults to the per-output-frequency period `T_ref(f) = 1/f`: the absolute broadband spectrum equals the Fourier transform times `f`, and an output at `f` does not depend on the other requested frequencies. An explicit `reference_frequency` instead gives the Fourier transform times that constant for every bin. Result metadata, statistics, port metadata and recording provenance retain `"per-frequency"` or the explicit float. The internal `PortDFTAccumulator.phasors(reference_frequency=None)` default follows the same per-bin rule (previously a fixed `1.0`). Source normalization consistently scales port V/I and powers alongside fields; `SpectralSampler(normalize_source=True)` is recommended for labels and optimisation. Normalized field sources and port excitations must share waveform parameters except amplitude; mismatches raise `ValueError` during time planning. Source-spectrum finalization evaluates analytic and tabulated waveforms and shared window weights with torch over the executed record. CW outputs remain bit-identical with source normalization disabled; source-normalized CW ports receive the consistent per-source-spectrum correction.

- Pure complex-CW FDTD uses consistent analytic quadratures and physical peak phasors. PlaneWave phase references the TF/SF box center with correct E/H export clocks. Independent absolute fields, copper work, ordinary material-gradient checks and eager/Graph active-window disk resume pass; complex cumulative joules are explicitly algorithmic two-bank work. Mixed complex CW/pulse and unsupported complex batch/spatial execution remain rejected.
- Bloch complex-field FDTD supports interior passive LTI R/L/C loads and SeriesRLC/ParallelRLC LumpedPort terminations with independent imaginary circuit history, combined complex V/I spectra and checkpoint/resume of both circuit banks. Driven ports, copper-terminal/two-sided foil coupling, seam-crossing voltage/current supports, nonlinear/modulated devices, MNA/networks and complex lumped adjoints remain unsupported in v1. Acceptance scripts check zero-k imaginary-state invariance and bounded source-path gaps without changing source providers, exact complex eager/Graph and disk-resume parity, pulse and analytic CW port/combined-field spectral normalization with maximum relative differences, carrier-CW power balance, strict lossy pulsed decay and bounded lossless field-plus-LC energy; broadband power residuals are diagnostic. A small source-free one-step spectral diagnostic includes both field/circuit banks and CPML memory, with open-cell, static-conductivity, no-CPML and complex-frequency-shift controls. Oblique fixed-k Bloch runs use band-scaled CFS-CPML by default; explicit alpha settings and real-field defaults are preserved. Acceptance includes native one-step spectra, propagating-wave reflection and actual Yee half-point grading checks. Lossless passivity, one-step spectra, reflection, carrier power balance and regression checks pass; lossy cases pass the 120k decay gate with a matched 1e-6 threshold for field-energy and current-squared ratios. The broadband TFSF injection limitation is tracked separately.

- Repeated physical mesh refinement preserves already declared SI port contour bounds verbatim, avoiding a second floating-point bounds round trip. Exact separated/combined PML24 inputs are prepared with explicit identities; their field execution and convergence remain pending.

- Predeclared 20/40 ns smooth startup controls suppress annular TE1 cavity ringing and reduce one-direction E/H absorption error to 0.0753%/0.0749%, with normalized copper heat error 0.0462%. Original four-run S failures remain recorded; complete scattering and mesh qualification with the final CW convention are still pending.

- Annular TE1 diagnostics preserve the 64.4% abrupt-CW S-absorption failure while independently matching normalized copper heat within 0.082%. Actual native time traces, complex128 GPU reductions and fixed-window FFT controls identify persistent cavity startup ringing; steady scattering and curved-geometry release qualification remain open.

- The completed independent 0.625 mm locally refined microstrip reference confirms the outer-grid approximation in both backends: independent uniform/graded Z and beta changes are 0.00644% and 0.00224%. Actual native/reference five-frequency transfer also passes; the finer continuum campaign remains running.

- The refined 0.625 mm microstrip Gaussian-drive record agrees with independent physical V/I transfer over all five frequencies and both central station pairs: maximum complex impedance and beta differences are 0.0141% and 0.00841%. Strict completeness/identity checks reject ten damaged records; the independent continuum impedance change remains 2.6574% and fails its 2% gate.

- Application campaigns retain every declared target frequency and independently reimport reference-frequency dielectric loss for each solve. The five saved families schedule 385 direct/import pairs with separate outputs and campaign-wide input/source identity checks; 37 CPU/GPU workflow controls pass. Full declared-band physical execution and convergence remain open.

- Printed-line workflows observe regional flux, dielectric heat and copper heat from sparse native CUDA samples through the shared physical-window loss API. Eight actual two-drive stripline controls cover PEC, bulk loss, copper and both losses; residuals relative to the small heat remain 3.29–4.73%, so attenuation and continuum qualification remain open.

- Refined known-current patch fields match an independent full-wave reference at 31 frequencies: maximum complex reflection difference 0.00796, impedance difference 1.58% and valid reflection-phase difference 0.795 degrees. Actual hard-PEC supports match exactly. Coarse failures remain recorded; final-source replay and complete independent convergence remain separate release gates.

- Physical 18/35 µm copper reference workflows retain skin-depth film calibration, actual PCB edge-grid preparation and short source-pinned native field resource pilots. Coarse failed heat controls remain recorded; full PCB equal-error convergence and formal surface/volume performance comparisons remain open.

- Physical coupled-line extraction uses four independent native E/H drives and power-dual common/differential variables to identify the full transfer matrix. The recorded coarse 1 GHz control retains signed attenuation and conditioning; full-band, continuum and conductor-loss qualification remain open.

- The third independent microstrip refinement retains complete 0.625 mm native E/H reference traces and an exact-grid Maxwell comparison: 1 GHz impedance/beta differences are 0.2643%/0.0908%. The independent 1.25→0.625 mm band impedance change is still 2.6574%, so continuum qualification remains open.

- Homogeneous TEM propagation constants use float64/complex128 phase arithmetic even when material and field arrays use float32/complex64. The original strict coax k0 gate and independent analytic lossy/graded controls pass without loosening their tolerances.

- Independent reference controls lower a declared known current to clipped native Cartesian or radial cylindrical edge supports with physical constitutive coefficients, without a distributed reference resistor. Actual vacuum/conductive single-edge experiments verify Ampere balance and the source midpoint clock; matched antenna response qualification follows separately.

- The canonical driven antenna near conductive tissue now runs through measured feed power, 1 W accepted-power SAR normalization, 1 g/10 g averaging and Result/SAR persistence, with eager/Graph equality. Its physical feed grid is explicit. The benchmark reports a functional workflow while retaining the independent field, mesh and absolute exposure qualification gap.

- Printed-dipole refinement preserves the actual magnetic feed midpoints. Fixed-air PML48 reaches 0.257% radiation closure; the 12/16 ns time control and x-only refinement pass, with the latter retaining 1.85% Fourier-integral radiated-power change. Known record-length normalization is explicit. Combined mesh, material and independent-reference qualification remain open; earlier failed boundary and mesh controls stay recorded.

- Fixed orthogonal bends export typed covariant finite/closed observations through physical world Huygens/NF2FF positions, cofactor areas and world phase. Independent analytic and native Graph/resume controls pass; the refined Cu PCB has 0.0559% NF2FF/flux and 0.0463% power residuals. Its 14% mesh power shift and missing external reference keep bent-antenna release qualification open. Unresolved Cu-interface interpolation requires future one-sided reconstruction and is explicitly rejected.

- Independent patch diagnostics now observe the full native electric displacement flux inside the declared H contour and reconstruct source branch current with the discrete time derivative. The executed native Ampere balance closes to 9.1e-7 relative error; external reflection convergence remains unqualified. Source resistance/phase controls consistently report reflection at a declared common 50-ohm reference.

- Full-suite regression maintenance checks supported in-domain FDFD PEC zero-field constraints and the live port E/H clock with complete runtime state fixtures. Truncated PEC iterations verify constraints separately from linear-solve convergence.

- Experimental fixed-curvature `BendCoordinates` execute through Scene/Simulation/Result with explicit covariant field frames, world-vector queries, physical conduction units, metric-integrated copper half cells and finite physical RF apertures. Actual source-work, RC/MNA/network, Graph/resume and axisymmetric TE/TM shell controls pass. Angular absorption, physical curved radiation, mapped gradients/batching/distribution and general cut-surface copper remain unqualified; the complete bent-antenna R2 workflow is open.

- Capability debt uses exact reviewed source identities with immutable baseline lineage, unchanged contracts and explicit negative-coverage/exit debt. New, changed and removed guards cannot hide behind scalar-budget cancellation; an inventory audit pass does not qualify unsupported physics or pending branch coverage.

- P2P spatial FDTD assembles native/sampled closed radiation faces and cross-shard mode observations, refreshes current-time observation halos, and supports atomic detached disk resume across fields/ADE/Cu/circuit/network-delay/wire spectra. Independent one/two-GPU finite-copper antenna physics and state parity are tested; scale performance remains open and small fixtures do not establish speedup.

- Finite curved-copper research has independent full-shell and planar-film references, separate physical E/H trace integrals and a passive GPU algebraic primitive. The unsplit-Yee spatial prototype fails transmission and absorption despite energy conservation; its failures are retained. Planar SurfaceMesh/CopperFoil sheets now use the separate conformal two-sided runtime (native CUDA and CPU reference backends), including faceted (piecewise-planar) meshes; smooth meshes with facets narrower than about a cell remain explicitly rejected. This is not curved-copper solver qualification.

- Automatic bulk conduction loss uses the same native Yee-edge conductivity as the FDTD update, retaining node averaging only for material families that use it in the forward solver. Independent staggered-interface work, sigma derivatives and native field-decay controls validate the correction; source-pinned offline campaigns can recompute loss from retained fields without rerunning or changing them.

- The independent printed-line reference workflow exports fixed physical geometry and exact grids with two exterior resistor feeds, native E/H probe addresses and recorded half-step timestamps. Executed coarse microstrip controls agree across 0.8–1.2 GHz within 0.105% in beta and 0.331% in complex impedance; the independent 1.25 mm refinement matches at 1 GHz within 0.094%/0.271%. Raw traces, source/build identities and immutable reuse controls are retained. Its 5.40% coarse-to-refined continuum impedance change remains outside the 2% mesh gate.

- Native TEM and quasi-TEM preparation supports graded transverse Yee metrics through a symmetric device finite-volume potential solve, physical energy and native gradients. Collocated modal observations integrate their actual cell widths; actual lossy/lossless GPU controls cover all axes, both directions and eager/Graph parity. Fixed-geometry CPW uniform/graded spectra agree within 0.020%; the refined1GHz physical impedance now passes2% analytic and adjacent-mesh controls, while full-band and loss qualification remain open. Other vector-mode families retain their spacing restrictions.

- Explicit material subsampling on graded grids spans the actual asymmetric Yee dual volume. Independent 50 µm layer-integral and conductivity-gradient controls prevent the old factor-of-two thin-layer bias; point sampling and uniform-grid quadrature retain their declared behavior.

- Good-conductor modal corrections now use the actual native split-Ampere half-cell normal-H curl and full Yee dual-face edge quadrature. An independent native-forcing oracle and frozen two-length Cu attenuation/phase controls validate these terms; physical forward foil dynamics remain unchanged and continuum edge-loss convergence is separately required.

- Printed-line qualification measures characteristic impedance from native PointMonitor E/H integrals and two-drive transfer eigenvectors, independently of modal z0. Refined stripline physical Z passes its 1 GHz control; frozen finite-Cu long-line spectra expose and check the native surface-forcing terms. CPW refinement, continuum conductor loss and complete full-band qualification remain open.

- Lumped/terminal magnetic-contour observers use RF current into the field, consistent with their positive E-line-integral voltage and active branch power. Independent all-axis Ampere/source-work controls and forward/gradient/batch regressions pass. Generic transverse-voltage/longitudinal-current WavePort orientation is preserved; a contour includes displacement current and need not equal a Norton branch.

- CopperFoil footprint ownership preserves bounded source/arithmetic ULPs at aligned whole-edge endpoints while rejecting real off-grid offsets. A second fixed physical printed-dipole benchmark declares PEC/35 µm Cu arms, a layered coupon and named coplanar finite-width feed; geometry, actual feed execution and full-band time/mesh/boundary controls are retained. Failed mesh and radiation gates remain open for physical qualification.

- The second printed dipole retains complete 12 ns, 41-frequency fine-grid, finite-copper, lossy-substrate and combined-loss executions with unchanged source identities and separate native-loss reductions. Positive loss channels and functional feeds are verified; adjacent mesh reflection and radiation closure failures remain explicit.

- Printed-dipole convergence fixtures hold the resolved PML endpoint spacings, physical feed/Huygens geometry and coupon cells while refining exterior vacuum independently. Actual 2/1/0.5 mm vacuum controls stabilize reflection and net flux; the remaining radiation residual and separate coupon-grid controls stay explicit.

- Multifrequency TerminalPort feeds use their native voltage/current spectra without implicitly storing a full-volume DFT. Named planar and thin-wire node/gap feeds preserve all final fields and port spectra bitwise under eager/Graph execution; explicitly requested full-field spectra remain available for loss calculations.

- The independent PCB reference workflow pins a private headless native build, dependency hashes, actual Yee geometry, physical pulse/time/PML settings and complex phasor conversion. Raw aperture and magnetic-contour controls retain failed comparisons and source identity; the current patch comparison remains outside the declared accuracy gate.

- Native checkpoint adjoints preserve finite-plane collocation, the exact physical-domain sample selection and area weights through Result reconstruction. Native flux fields, coordinates, widths and integrated power agree with ordinary forward results. Fixed-topology CopperFoil thickness/conductivity gradients have complete native/sampled Huygens radiation-power finite-difference, normal-forward and captured-checkpoint controls; these finite-horizon derivatives do not assert antenna convergence or geometry gradients.

- Standalone finite native/sampled observation faces integrate exact intersections with their declared physical rectangles, including partial cells with exterior centers. Differentiable midpoint field reconstruction preserves float64 geometry, original field precision and component collocation metadata. Independent area/affine-flux, large-offset and actual copper radiation-gradient controls pass; the printed-dipole radiation residual remains unqualified.

- TerminalPort resolves named PEC Boxes, real PlanarPEC polygons/holes and supported CopperFoil supports, with explicit `current_surface` and `voltage_aperture` for coplanar feeds. Physical contact, return components, every parallel gap path and native Yee connectivity are audited; mesh-created shorts are rejected. The audit persists in PortData. Zero-area contacts, unsupported rotations and active split-Cu electric feed rows require separate support.

- Fixed real isotropic PEC `SurfaceMesh` geometry supports true clipped conforming surface-current constraints on native GPU Yee fields, with exact aligned PlanarPEC reduction. Eager/Graph, private native batch, disk resume and point classification are tested. A fixed faceted sphere passes its refined 5% independent scattering gate and oblique/bent source-off million-step energy controls. Finite curved copper, local ADE, tensor/Bloch fields, adjoints, distributed ownership and overlapping feeds remain explicit unsupported combinations; R2 qualification remains open.
- Planar and piecewise-planar (faceted: bent/folded boards, closed prisms, faceted cylinders; folds up to 90 degrees, facets at least about a cell wide) `SurfaceMesh` thin sheets at arbitrary tilt (CopperFoil, or PEC with `sheet_model="conformal_two_sided"`) run on a conformal two-sided cut complex. Independent per-side chord fields use a locally implicit Newmark update (beta = 1/4), sparse 2x2 block-Jacobi Chebyshev solve and the existing passive copper two-port coupling the two sides of conductor patches. The explicit operator and leapfrog bound equal the PEC complex’s; the chord mass factor is 0.5. Native CUDA graph/eager execution is bit-identical, and single-step private float64 CUDA/CPU parity is within 2.63e-16. A 32-facet 35 um copper cylinder at lambda/40 matches the Bessel series to 0.343 % (TE) / 0.425 % (TM) and the PEC sheet to 3.61e-5 / 6.27e-5, passing the declared accuracy gates. Chord operator storage is below 739 B/trace excluding modal state; captured-step cost gates remain unqualified (shared-GPU ratios 2.991x at 6188 traces and 6.265x at 61342 traces miss the 1.5x/2x targets). The chord integrator adds second-order temporal dispersion; measured accuracy, dispersion and captured-step costs are recorded in `docs/plans/fdtd-3d-thin-sheet-runtime.md` and the linked trace-basis plan. The CPU reference entry remains `Simulation.fdtd(..., backend="cpu_reference")`. Smooth meshes with sub-cell facets, periodic boundaries, ports other than local sheet gap feeds, sources on the sheet, gradients, batch and distribution are rejected explicitly. Sheet gap feeds and their qualified checkpoint/resume path are described below.

- Finite conformal thin-sheet rims split a Yee face only when both sheet-chord endpoints lie inside the outline. Partial rim chords leave ordinary faces for air circulation around the sheet end, with the existing segment masses and magnetic floor. This removes the earlier excess rim capacitance (strips resonated about 4-7% low, non-converging): a finite strip now converges to the native reference as O(h) (+1.1% at lambda/120), long-strip waves travel at c at mid-cell height, and in-plane rotation spread equals the native PlanarPEC staircase (about 3% at lambda/120). Sub-cell height dependence of 1.6-2.4% remains from the small-cut magnetic floor. The rotation gate is defined as no worse than native PlanarPEC.

- WaveModeSpec accepts explicit `conductor_model="good_conductor"` for a bounded first-order TEM/quasi-TEM finite-copper expansion. Actual CopperFoil fields retain both constitutive traces; modal launch keeps its documented PEC-limit shape. Per-frequency band, skin-depth, impedance and propagation criteria, approximation metadata, all-axis captured execution and persistence are validated. Independent physical conductor attenuation remains a separate gate.

- Finite-plane and closed-box FDTD monitors accept optional physical `sample_spacing`, with native Yee interpolation and exact midpoint area weights independent of field resolution. GPU grouped/Graph sampling, native batch, disk resume, persistence and material adjoints share the same weights. Native full planes remain the default; radiation quadrature requires separate convergence. P2P distributed sampling uses uniquely owned global physical quadrature and refreshed observation halos.

- Fixed physical printed-line campaigns now retain asymmetric four-channel mode conversion, independent aperture and launch-distance controls, and full two-length ABCD propagation eigenvalues. Homogeneous lossy stripline/CPW attenuation and phase pass independent analytic controls; coarse impedance and finite-copper qualification remain separate.

- Material-separated two-length line controls preserve identical physical observations and explicit PEC, dielectric-loss and finite-copper variants. Eight CPU compilations, 21 contracts and 11 execution-admission checks pass; twelve full-duration field controls and attenuation qualification remain pending.

- Experimental scalar harmonic balance now admits bodies with both strictly positive electric and magnetic DC conductivities through the public Scene/Simulation/Result path. `Result.query_dc_dual` exposes E, H, both scalar potential parts and the interior magnetic flux pole residue; interior finite DC B remains unavailable. DC H point monitors, live source-response gradients and schema-14 exact preparation/state restoration are supported. Physical accuracy, harmonic truncation and production qualification remain open.

- Native FDTD checkpoint adjoints propagate scalar electric permittivity and conductivity through conductive and electric-ADE field/port and MNA coupling. Direct Scene tensors and SceneModule parameters retain gradients, with Graph and checkpoint-stride parity, finite-difference step scans, and normal-forward optimization checks. Two-sided foil and topology gradients require separate qualification.

- Detached single-GPU FDTD resume preserves full-field DFT, point/plane spectral monitors and field/flux time traces through disk checkpoints. Observation configuration, buffers and active real-field DFT counters are validated against the saved execution step before restoration; static Box/PlanarPEC material-cache entries own private writable copies, with actual Graph, native batch and persistence parity tests. Copper-foil execution/cache qualification is tracked separately.

- Real-field spectral-tail Graph preparation respects the remaining sampling horizon, including short runs and nonzero copper states. Short-run and disk-resume regressions, native spectral/time products and two-GPU copper radiation pass a fresh 56-case combined GPU check; complete physical convergence remains separate.

- Ordinary real-float32 FDTD spectral point/plane monitors reuse native grouped frequency sampling inside a CUDA Graph, preserving source normalization and E/H half-step phases. Result reports actual observer capture; eager/Graph checkpoints interchange observation state. Five interleaved 31-frequency antenna measurements preserve all outputs bitwise and reduce median run time from 247.23 to 4.025 seconds. One field group at up to two frequencies retains existing native point scheduling to avoid grouped-launch overhead; final legacy performance requalification remains required.

- The restored probe-fed patch passes nominal matching, broadside radiation and 3% power-closure checks in its actual resonant band. A fixed physical sheet/probe/finite-aperture patch fixture preserves geometry across independent mesh studies; complete convergence and finite-copper qualification remain separate gates.

- Conformal thin-sheet gather/scatter plans use one native kernel per call on CUDA, preserving exact field casts and private FP64 index copies inside captured graphs. Copper right-hand-side assembly, chord forcing, and state/loss updates use FP64 native kernels with deterministic loss reduction. Acx/Acc row sums use prepare-time selection of 1/2/4/8 cooperating lanes per matrix and a fixed shuffle tree, preserving per-element affine rounding and reusing the existing coupling buffer. All CSR plans read by fused sheet kernels use int32 row pointers and column indices. GPU gates are pending. `WITWIN_MAXWELL_THIN_SHEET_KERNEL=torch` selects the unchanged tensor reference; CPU execution always uses that reference. The predictor, pair solves and feed source/diagnostic loops retain their tensor arithmetic.
- Conformal thin-sheet host compilation batches edge/face classification and native edge assignment, and caches per-node dual extents while preserving output bits and ordering. PEC feeds reuse their first chord build when no exact on-sheet traces change the mass inputs; spectral solves remain unchanged. `scripts/dev/fdtd/thin_sheet_setup_cpu.py` profiles CPU setup and checks every compiled output against a separately imported archive of master. GPU gates pending.
- Finite-thickness CopperFoil couples two planar electric traces through passive diffusion states, with explicit PEC contacts, surface heat/power accounting, actual CUDA Graph execution and disk resume. Physical thickness does not set a grid interval. Model bands and unsupported local couplings are explicit; oblique/long-run and combined material-ADE qualifications are tracked separately.
- CopperFoil setup reuses automatic modal fits in a process-local cache keyed by exact material values, parameter dtype/device, frequency band and fit options, returning private response tensors. Sorted integer ownership checks and array membership replace per-edge Python walks. `scripts/dev/fdtd/copper_setup_bitid.py` profiles CPU setup and compares all compiled copper and initialized copper-state values against an archived master; GPU gates are pending.
- Automatic CopperFoil diffusion uses a passive paired-pole fit targeting 5e-5 in-band accuracy and extended-band error bounded at about 2x the reference series, with exact DC moments and odd integrator: 35 µm uses 47 states over 0.1–10 GHz (previously 513) and 27 over 1–6 GHz (previously 257); in-band DFT observables use in-band admittance, while broadband time-domain quantities depend on both bands; explicit mode_count retains the differentiable exact-pole series.
- CopperFoil free edges (`edge_model="auto"`, the default `CopperFoil.DEFAULT_EDGE_MODEL`) carry a finite-thickness edge impedance: each rim trace whose in-plane neighbour edge is neither foil, conductor/attachment contact (PEC, conformal PEC, good conductors, wires, ports, lumped elements) nor a non-periodic boundary has its two-face impedance scaled by a geometric factor from the rectangular-edge stopping distance t/(4 pi e^pi) and tabulated discrete Yee half-plane constants, blended in smoothly over 2-4 skin depths at the band's upper edge. Loss and internal inductance stay consistent; port good-conductor modes, heat/spectral reports (`free_edge_trace_count`, per-trace `edge_impedance_factor`, gathered across shards) and thickness/conductivity gradients include it. A 35 µm stripline matches an independent cross-section reference to +3.7/+0.9/-0.1% at 2.5/1.25/0.625 mm (bare rim: -38/-35/-31%); strips under 4 cells and slots under 3 cells warn. `edge_model="none"` keeps the bare zero-thickness rim.
- Behavior change: CopperFoil free-edge impedance (`edge_model='auto'`) is on by default; existing thick-foil scenes with free edges change Cu loss; use `edge_model='none'` for the legacy bare-rim model. The default lives in `CopperFoil.DEFAULT_EDGE_MODEL`. Numbers that change: benchmark scenes `benchmark/scenes/rf/printed_lines.py` (`metal="copper"` stripline/CPW/coupled pair: Cu attenuation rises ~1.6-2x toward the independent reference), `benchmark/scenes/antenna/printed_dipole.py` and `printed_patch.py` (35 µm arms/patch), PCB adapter scenes (`witwin/maxwell/adapters/pcb.py` thick copper layers) and the dev campaigns built on them (`scripts/dev/fdtd/pcb_application_campaign.py`, `pcb_copper_performance.py`, `bend_rf_radiation_control.py`); frozen printed-line/antenna artifacts recorded before this change keep their bare-rim values. Tests whose expectations encode the factor: `tests/rf/wave_validation/test_good_conductor_modes.py` (modal surface work includes kappa at the strip rims), `tests/materials/surface_impedance/test_copper_foil_edge.py`; fixture schema updates in `test_copper_foil_material_ade.py` and `tests/fdtd/multi_gpu/test_copper_report_gather.py`. Foils thinner than 2 skin depths at their upper band edge (most surface-impedance unit tests) and edgeless foils are unchanged (bit-identical).

- Fixed-topology real-field CopperFoil adjoints propagate both electric traces, diffusion history and cumulative heat to scalar thickness/conductivity tensors with explicit mode_count. Result conductor energies retain PyTorch gradients; independent finite-difference scans, actual Graph/checkpoint parity and a true-copper skin-depth case pass. Local bulk-ADE derivatives also pass. Fixed-topology periodic and mixed Bloch/CPML foil gradients pass finite-difference and captured-checkpoint controls; geometry derivatives remain unqualified.

- Fixed planar copper spectral surface and adjacent bulk loss retain native checkpoint gradients in copper thickness/conductivity and static bulk permittivity/conductivity, including direct loss prefactors, both independent side spectra and reusable RF batch tapes. Independent forward finite-difference scans, actual windows/Graph and Bloch controls pass; detached side-spectrum gradients remain rejected.

- Quasi-static FDTD line-mode potentials, energies and transverse fields use the existing device-native electrostatic PCG solver. Native longitudinal PlanarPEC ownership participates in port cross-sections, and modal geometry preserves original coordinate precision through aperture, voltage and current integration. Fixed physical transmission-line convergence remains separately qualified.

- FDTD preparation diagnostics report adjacent-cell grading, per-axis vacuum-CFL contributions and effective interval coverage across declared thin dielectric Box dimensions. These measurements expose under-resolution without asserting physical adequacy.

- TEM and quasi-TEM modes retain native Yee electric differences and vacuum-capacitance magnetic currents through sampling and power normalization. Homogeneous modes also use residual-qualified PCG, support two-conductor common/differential potentials, and derive V/I from the declared contour without replacing it by a power identity. Independent capacitance and contour-conservation controls pass.

- Complete Bloch TFSF slab faces deposit each native periodic representative once; explicit zero-phase mixed Bloch/PML boundaries are supported. Actual empty-scene CW/pulse controls cover all three axes and both directions. A settled dielectric grating now passes empty-reference energy conservation without an expected-failure exemption; absolute analytic-source amplitude qualification remains separate.
- Complex CW Bloch/TFSF injection and functional adjoint replay now share exp(i k·r − i omega t), matching positive Floquet translation and the spectral convention. Independent vacuum and 35-degree TE/TM foil controls pass; thin/thick foil cases also complete one million field updates each with bounded energy and nonnegative heat.

- CopperFoil interfaces jointly eliminate local Debye/Drude/Lorentz electric polarization with both surface traces at implicit midpoint, retaining separate material histories, passive storage and heat. Magnetic poles keep their native H histories. Independent ODE/energy, actual Graph/resume and foil/material finite-difference gradient checks pass; batched and Bloch combinations remain separately qualified.

- Native GPU Batch advances direct lumped/terminal RF drives, passive RLC loads, copper foil traces and electric/magnetic Debye/Drude/Lorentz histories in the ordinary solver order. Device waveform/spectral tables and reusable Graph buffers preserve repeated runs and freeze completed lanes; independent fields, complex V/I/a/b and heat agree. Native batch also advances fixed-parameter MNA circuits and passive dynamic networks with prepared waveform/switch schedules, reusable factors and independent gradient checks. Unsupported nonlinear and wire couplings remain explicit.

- Static CopperFoil and electric/magnetic dispersive material banks participate in the bounded FDTDMaterialCache. Keys cover support ownership, thickness, conductivity, band/order and pole parameters; every consumer constructs private evolving histories. Cache/uncached fields and loss match, with corruption isolation and physical-input invalidation tests.

- Fixed physical thin-PEC microstrip, stripline, coplanar and coupled-pair benchmark scenes preserve dimensions and physical PML thickness across refinements. Two-length reports distinguish measured propagation, impedance, conditioning and passivity; initial stripline phase/power controls pass while coarse impedance remains explicitly unqualified.

- RF network sweeps preserve conductor losses by excitation through Result.conductor_loss_columns, with scalar energies and frequency-resolved powers per foil. The network result has no last-column aggregate loss. Independent column, cache and disk-persistence checks pass.

- FDTD RF port clocks and native GPU waveform tables retain float64 physical time independently of float32 fields. Long-duration carrier, ordinary/batch RF and disk-resume controls preserve the authoritative Yee step.

- A reproducible five-family numerical application campaign compares direct Scene declarations with imported finite-aperture inputs over 12 ns, retaining exact compiled/complex-output parity, source fingerprints and raw artifact hashes. Original drafts remain distinct from qualified antenna candidates.

- All five complete untuned real-loss application candidates pass 3% power closure with explicitly enlarged air and 16-layer PML, while direct/imported outputs remain bitwise identical. Separate failed boundary, duration and quadrature controls are retained; material/grid convergence and hosted deployment remain unqualified. PCB import preserves an explicitly declared material-quadrature policy without changing geometry.

- PCB input ports may declare physical `current_contour_bounds_m` so normal-grid refinement preserves the magnetic reference loop. Native geometry validation still checks its plane and topology; omitted contours retain the closest enclosing native-loop policy.

- The application refinement driver prepares independently controlled normal/lateral grids using odd interval subdivisions, fixed physical conductor/material geometry, retained node/half-plane references and explicit material quadrature. Prepared inputs are separate from executed convergence qualification.

- Dynamic dielectric-breakdown feedback is rejected with coupled RF ports because latched conductivity changes require rebuilding the joint field/circuit response. Fixed static conductive coupling remains supported; circuit-driven stress-only controls retain their existing path.

- Numerical PCB imports preserve explicitly declared finite CopperFoil thickness, conductivity and band on planar supports. Unsupported implicit solid-layer collapse, unknown conductor models and accidental loss disabling are rejected; native lossy finite-aperture execution is covered.

- Fixed physical printed-patch fixtures support independent substrate, lateral-edge, PML, padding and duration controls plus explicit PEC/copper and lossy-substrate variants. Raw convergence comparisons retain failures; final combined-grid and independent-reference qualification remain separate.

- Printed-line fixtures can refine transverse mode geometry independently of axial spacing while preserving physical conductors, reference planes and PML width. Independent homogeneous conformal impedances converge below 2% for the finest recorded stripline and coplanar cross-sections; complete propagating/lossy line qualification remains separate.

- Two-device spatial FDTD gives planar copper/ADE traces unique owners and exchanges sparse high-side electric halos for cross-partition magnetic curls. All three orientations, CPML, graded grids, material histories, remote MNA/network ports and saved loss metadata match single-GPU controls. NCCL, direct unbound drives, wire mixing, distributed adjoints/resume and radiation remain explicit separate limits.

- Distributed planar-copper reports gather complete uniquely owned global side spectra and surface-power tiles, preserving sampling context and typed data through radiation reduction and disk resume. Original finite-copper patch/circuit controls pass on two GPUs; duplicate owners and incompatible sampling contexts are rejected. General geometry and mesh convergence remain separate qualifications.

- FDTD PowerLossMonitor automatically reports passive bulk electric-polarization work and replaces native shared-trace conduction with physical left/right foil-side integrals. Retained Result spectra preserve Graph, batch, resume, normalization and disk persistence, including complex Bloch fields. Uncoupled electric-source deposition onto an active foil trace and unavailable spectral gradients fail explicitly.

- All five retained application families now have explicit complete untuned candidate inputs with full 12 ns direct/imported complex-port parity. Separate 18/35 um copper inputs save their physical layer-collapse map, preserve substrate thickness and expose radiation/bulk/conductor power controls; convergence and qualification remain explicit gates.

- Homogeneous isotropic TEM wave ports support static electric/magnetic loss with internal PEC conductors, actual Yee complex propagation, complex CW profiles and physical V/I power-wave conversion. Independent two-length attenuation/phase and Result persistence pass; spatially varying lossy fills and poles remain explicit separate limits.

This document tracks the current user-visible capabilities of the `maxwell` package.

Release 0.5.1 introduces the experimental Batch execution tier and FDTD correctness repairs described in [the release notes](docs/reference/release-notes-0.5.1.md). Existing ordinary-simulation capabilities retain their documented scope; Batch support is specified separately below.

- Package installation requires `witwin>=0.4,<0.5` and uses its public `PhysicalMaterial` contract; the dependency range resolves against published base-package versions.
- Release wheels declare their plotting/progress/notebook display import dependencies. Publishing validates package imports outside the checkout and the native Batch allocator symbols across the platform/ABI matrix.

- Batch tuning isolates copyable `SceneModule` parameters, buffers and Python attributes per trial, preserves shared tensor storage, and starts each candidate from the same CPU/CUDA RNG state. Only the final ordinary preparation updates the caller's module state. Trial copies and scene materialization on another execution device use that device's private memory account. Arbitrary external side effects and allocations on producer devices outside the execution policy are outside this isolation/accounting contract.

- `python -m benchmark batch --scaling-audit WORKLOAD --devices cuda:0 cuda:1 --variants N --concurrency B --update-report` interleaves complete streamed runs on each GPU and all GPUs, including CPU output readiness, and checks spectra against independent simulations. Raw artifacts retain all timing samples and competing compute-process observations. The matching two-device 1.7x gate requires numerical agreement and observed exclusive compute access; foreign processes or sampler errors prevent acceptance. Periodic sampling cannot prove complete exclusivity or exclude display activity. Idle prepared comparison modes remain resident, so this workflow does not measure isolated memory use.
