- `Material.relative_permeability(frequency)` for isotropic magnetic dispersion evaluation
- Instantaneous isotropic Kerr nonlinearity on `Material(kerr_chi3=...)`
- Instantaneous second- and third-order nonlinearity through `NonlinearSusceptibility(chi2=..., chi3=...)` composed via `Material(nonlinearity=...)` (single descriptor or a tuple): `chi2` adds the per-component `P_i = eps0 * chi2 * E_i^2` (second-harmonic generation, optical rectification) and `chi3` the isotropic `P_i = eps0 * chi3 * |E|^2 * E_i`, lowering to exactly the same runtime channel as `Material(kerr_chi3=...)` (the two are equivalent and additive, validated bitwise against the Kerr fast path); multiple descriptors sum, and nonlinear media may coexist with dispersive or anisotropic materials in other structures of the same scene (FDTD only)
- A single `Material` may carry both instantaneous nonlinearity (`chi2` / `chi3` / `TwoPhotonAbsorption`) and electric dispersive poles (`Debye` / `Drude` / `Lorentz`), the combination required for `chi2` second-harmonic generation where the dispersion sets `n(w)` vs `n(2w)` and hence the phase mismatch `dk = (2w/c) * (n(2w) - n(w))`: the runtime subtracts the ADE polarization current against the same field-dependent effective permittivity `eps_eff = eps_lin + eps0 * (chi2 * E_i + chi3 * |E|^2)` that the nonlinear displacement-current term uses (so the instantaneous nonlinear index shift and the dispersive response stay consistent), validated against the analytic `sinc(dk*L/2)` phase-matching factor (linear-in-L phase-matched SHG growth vs Maker-fringe suppression in a mismatched dispersive slab); this same-material combination is forward-only, its FDTD adjoint stays guarded (FDTD only)
- Two-photon absorption through `TwoPhotonAbsorption(beta, n0=None)` composed via `Material(nonlinearity=...)`: the TPA coefficient `beta` [m/W] of `dI/dz = -beta * I^2` enters the FDTD update as a field-dependent conductivity `sigma_NL(x, t) = (2/3) * beta * (n0 * eps0 * c0)^2 * |E(x, t)|^2` folded into the semi-implicit lossy decay term per step (the 2/3 factor matches the cycle-averaged dissipation of a linearly polarized real CW field to `beta * I^2`); `n0` defaults to `sqrt(eps_r)` of the host material, multiple descriptors sum, and TPA composes freely with `chi2`/`chi3` and static conductivity in the same material (qualified by independent cycle-work units, native coefficient/gradient and Graph/resume controls; original-input pulse-energy trends use a measured identical-drive linear-slab reference)
- Convenience constructors `Material.debye(...)`, `Material.drude(...)`, and `Material.lorentz(...)`
- Experimental linear-gain media via gain-signed (negative oscillator strength, `delta_eps < 0`) `LorentzPole` / `Material.lorentz(...)`, gated behind an explicit `allow_gain=True` opt-in so an accidental sign flip still raises; opting in emits a `warnings.warn` stability notice because gain media can violate the usual FDTD Courant/stability margins
- Space-time permittivity modulation through `ModulationSpec(frequency, amplitude, phase=0.0)` composed via `Material(modulation=...)`: the static permittivity becomes `eps(x, t) = eps_static(x) * (1 + amplitude(x) * cos(2*pi*frequency*t + phase(x)))` for non-reciprocal devices, isolators, and frequency conversion; `amplitude` (modulation depth in `[0, 0.5)`) and `phase` [rad] are scalars or 3D grids mapped over the structure's `Box` extent (same node-coverage/trilinear-resampling convention as `MaterialRegion.density`, enabling traveling-wave `phase(x)` profiles), the compiler derives absolute modulation quadratures with the static component averaging rule (arithmetic tangentially, the first-order harmonic or tensor variation normally, including binary and smooth Kottke windows), then normalizes by static edge permittivity so partial cells retain the correct constitutive variation and fully interior edges retain their existing quadratures; automatic timestep bounds use these same component modulation amplitudes, and coefficient preparation remains valid inside `torch.inference_mode()`, and the FDTD runtime applies the charge-conserving update `E_new = decay * (m_prev/m_next) * E_old + (curl/m_next) * curl(H)` inside dedicated native CUDA E-update kernel variants (`updateElectricFieldE*Modulated3D` plus dense- and compressed/slab-CPML counterparts) driven by a per-edge modulation angular-frequency field plus a persistent FP64 endpoint clock derived from a device integer step counter, with FP64 phase range reduction before FP32 trigonometry (so no coefficient tensors or host phase scalars are rebuilt per step, CUDA Graph capture remains valid, and a single Scene may hold several distinct modulation frequencies at once — each modulated structure stamps its own angular frequency onto the cells it covers, letting disjoint structures modulate independently); a modulated slab reproduces the expected `omega +/- Omega` sidebands in the transmitted spectrum, two disjoint slabs driven at `Omega_A` and `Omega_B` produce both sideband pairs at their own frequencies, and the compressed (slab) CPML memory mode reproduces the dense CPML modulated run bit-for-bit
- Space-time modulation composes with electric/magnetic dispersion (`Debye` / `Drude` / `Lorentz` poles) and with the instantaneous nonlinear channels (`kerr_chi3` / `chi2` / `TwoPhotonAbsorption`) in the same `Material` or across separate structures in a Scene (the electro-optic-modulator edge, e.g. a Pockels-modulated dispersive crystal): the scalar modulation factor `m(x, t)` scales the `eps_inf` background only, so the ADE polarization current is folded through the same per-step `1/m_next` factor as `curl(H)` (a dedicated native CUDA `applyPolarizationCurrentModulated3D` kernel divides the dispersive current by `eps_inf * m_next`, reducing bit-for-bit to the plain subtraction where the modulation depth vanishes), and the field-dependent nonlinear decay/curl coefficients feed the modulated E update directly; the modulated dispersive solve reduces to the pure-dispersive solve as the depth vanishes (within `O(depth)`), generates the `omega +/- Omega` sidebands on top of a dispersive slab, converts a smaller fraction of carrier to sidebands than a non-dispersive medium of the same static index (the poles are not modulated), and the modulated Kerr solve reduces to the pure-modulation solve as `chi3` vanishes; this combination is FDTD forward-only, its adjoint stays guarded, and a modulated anisotropic tensor or a modulated static conductivity remains rejected with a physics-worded error
- The pairwise composability of the FDTD material and environmental features is a documented, validated contract (`tests/materials/combinations/test_combination_matrix.py`, the P5.2 combination matrix): across the material axes {dispersive, diagonal-aniso, full-aniso, nonlinear (`chi2` / `chi3` / `TwoPhotonAbsorption`), modulated, `sigma_e`} plus the environmental axes {Bloch boundary, CPML overlap}, every physically meaningful ordered pair compiles and runs to finite fields, while the combinations still out of reach raise a physics-worded error (never "not implemented yet") instead of producing silently wrong physics — nonlinear / full-aniso / modulated media under complex Bloch fields (the real-valued update kernels are undefined on phase-shifted complex fields), a modulated or nonlinear anisotropic permittivity tensor (would need a per-step re-inversion of a coupled 3x3 tensor), a modulated static conductivity, and a full off-diagonal tensor overlapping a split-field (non-CPML) PML. Diagonal and full anisotropy are not an independent pair: a permittivity is either diagonal or full and both occupy the single `epsilon_tensor` slot
- `Material.sellmeier(b_coefficients, c_coefficients, eps_inf=1.0, ...)` for lossless Sellmeier dielectrics (for example Schott BK7 glass), which lowers each `B_i * lambda^2 / (lambda^2 - C_i)` term to a zero-damping `LorentzPole` with `delta_eps = B_i` and `resonance_frequency = c / sqrt(C_i)`; `c_coefficients` are the squared resonance wavelengths in SI `meters^2`
- Frequency-dependent material evaluation through the shared material compiler for single-frequency workflows, including isotropic `sigma_e`
- Component-aware material compilation keeps scalar summary tensors for visualization / compatibility while exposing explicit per-axis material grids for solver backends and `Result.material(...)`
- Density-based `MaterialRegion` compilation using native PyTorch tensor interpolation, optional box filtering, and optional projection
- Vacuum background by default (`eps_r = 1`, `mu_r = 1`)
- Multi-structure material composition on the scene grid
- Structure overlap resolution uses `Structure.priority` first, then append order among equal-priority structures
- Occupancy-based material blending on the scene grid, with SDF-driven soft occupancy for shared `Box`, `Sphere`, `Cylinder`, `Torus`, and `HollowBox`
- Phase-1.5 primitive SDF coverage for shared `Ellipsoid`, `Cone`, `Pyramid`, and `Prism`
- Differentiable SDF occupancy for `PolySlab` and `ComplexPolySlab`, with gradients through polygon vertices, axis bounds, and sidewall angle
- Differentiable mesh occupancy compilation through core's PyTorch signed-distance evaluation, with geometry-state-aware triangle caching and shared surface-band modes. Fixed solid-mesh subpixel coverage batches finite-difference queries only in the fractional-fill band; watertight meshes and meshes with exactly cancelling directed boundary edges prune queries outside padded world-space bounds. Nonperiodic uniform-grid compilation reuses coverage at bit-identical node/Yee sample coordinates within one compile. FDFD and FDTD now share one material discretisation: electric Yee-edge eps/sigma_e and magnetic H-face mu/sigma_m. FDFD's matrix, local responses, preconditioner and material pullback consume these shared samples with its existing exp(-i omega t) loss convention. This is a consistency change, not an accuracy gain at about 16 cells per wavelength.
- Supersampled voxel averaging for smoother material interfaces on partial cells
- Opt-in `SubpixelSpec(averaging="kottke_edge", samples=4)` evaluates binary occupancy in each Yee E-edge/H-face window, estimates the normal from the in-window real-material first moment, and blends the physical diagonal between harmonic and arithmetic means for scalar isotropic `eps` and `mu`. FDTD and FDFD share the resulting diagonals. Geometry and MaterialRegion tensor fields must be fixed (trainable tensors raise); Structure material values retain their PyTorch path. Conductivity and dispersive weights retain arithmetic coverage averaging, anisotropic windows retain local polarized treatment, and sheets/surface-impedance/full-tensor models retain the node-based fallback. PCB conductivity replay rejects this mode. Interface-band and mesh-bound pruning limit supersampling. Existing explicit averaging modes are unchanged. External-reference error on cropped/scaled p16s0 is 6.06/7.03%, 4.14/4.86%, 3.31/3.97% (shell/inside, ppw16/24/32), within 0.264% of A's error and about 1.8-2.0 times lower than polarized. The epsilon_r 4 sphere remains second order and improves at ppw32 (2.80/2.45%); ppw16/24 errors are higher than polarized, so uniform nonregression across resolutions is not claimed.
- `SubpixelSpec(averaging="kottke_smooth", samples=4)` is the recommended differentiable subpixel mode for geometry and Structure material-value optimization in FDTD and FDFD; MaterialRegion density gradients are not qualified for this mode. It shares the per-Yee E-edge/H-face windows, first-moment normals and physical-diagonal blend with `kottke_edge`, using curvature-corrected fractional coverage instead of binary samples. Conductivity, poles and anisotropic constituents retain the same treatment; PCB conductivity replay rejects the mode. On measured smooth/mesh geometry it is comparable to `kottke_edge`, and it improves on polarized in 15 of 16 measured region/level entries (the exception is G1-C's ppw20 shell). On sharp high-contrast edges and corners it does not match `kottke_edge`: error reaches 1.61 times the binary mode's error. Unresolved experiment levels remain unqualified.
- Polarized (Kottke normal-projection) subpixel averaging for `eps` and `mu` (inverse-tensor diagonal; requires `samples > 1`), with interface normals from the gradient of the sub-sample-averaged material and coverage-fill normals from central differences of the signed distance (differentiable in geometry parameters). It reduces normal-field error at high-contrast interfaces
- In-domain perfect-electric-conductor material via `Material.pec(name=None)` / `Material.is_pec`, compiled to a differentiable union PEC occupancy grid and enforced in FDTD through per-edge open-fraction coefficient scaling (`staircase` hard edge, or `conformal` partial fill from the per-edge geometric coverage fraction, which reduces to the `staircase` mask bit for bit wherever the conductor surface does not cut the edge). The `staircase` short set is node-consistent: an E edge is shorted iff both endpoints are conductor nodes (inside or on any PEC body), so a union of abutting bodies is one conductor and a curved or off-grid surface leaves no zero-E edge hanging off an exterior node. A vacuum round coax (cylinder or node-centred box union) then carries its TEM wave at the same speed as a grid-aligned square coax (0.99998c at 0.25 mm, 5 GHz), where the former two-node-average rule gave 0.83-0.93c; grid-aligned conductors keep the identical edge set. FDFD eliminates the same staircase edge set
- Zero-thickness conductive 2D sheets via `Medium2D(sigma_s=...)` [S]: attached to a `Structure` whose geometry is an axis-aligned `Box` with exactly one zero-size axis, the sheet snaps to the nearest node plane along its normal and lowers to the equivalent volumetric conductivity `sigma_s / dcell` on the two tangential Yee-edge conductivity components of that single layer (the normal component is untouched); sheet contributions are additive with bulk conductivity and other sheets, and `Medium2D.sheet_conductivity(omega)` / `sheet_conductivity_at_freq(f)` expose the analytic sheet conductivity for validation
- `Graphene(chemical_potential=..., scattering_time=..., temperature=300.0, include_interband=False)` 2D sheet with the Kubo surface conductivity (`chemical_potential` in eV, `temperature` in K, `scattering_time` in s): the intraband (Drude-like) term `sigma_s(omega) = A / (1/tau - i*omega)` lowers to a tangential-only Drude pole on the snapped Yee layer (`eps0*omega_p^2 = A/dcell`); with `include_interband=True` the T>0 principal-value interband term (`hbar*omega -> 2*|mu_c|` absorption edge) is fitted at construction to a small set of Lorentz sheet terms (`delta_eps = strength / (eps0*dcell)`) and lowered to tangential Lorentz poles, matching the analytic Kubo conductivity to within a few percent across the optical band; both are advanced by the existing native CUDA Drude/Lorentz ADE kernels, and the intraband rate plus interband resonances feed the FDTD auto-`dt` bound
- `Medium2D.sheet_lorentz_terms()` exposes resonant `(strength, omega_0, gamma)` surface-conductivity terms (each `-i*omega*strength*omega_0^2/(omega_0^2 - omega^2 - i*gamma*omega)` [S]); unlike Drude sheet terms these represent a capacitive below-edge reactance (`Im(sigma) < 0`) and lower to volumetric Lorentz poles, and are additive with the static and Drude sheet channels in `sheet_conductivity(omega)`
- `LossyMetalMedium(conductivity=...)` good-conductor with a normal-incidence surface-impedance (Leontovich) boundary runtime: the metal interior is masked and each step the two tangential E faces are updated from the vacuum-side tangential H via the resistive Leontovich relation `E_t = R * (n x H)`, with the surface resistance `R = sqrt(omega0*mu0/(2*sigma))` evaluated at the operating frequency (the reactive part of `Z_s` is intentionally omitted: its explicit time-derivative overwrite is non-passive and unstable, and for a good conductor it shifts `|Gamma|` by `< 1.3e-4`); coverage is evaluated against the physical `Domain.bounds`, so a half-space that ends at the physical boundary remains valid when `PreparedScene` appends external PML cells, and either low-side or high-side illuminated faces use the actual geometry-face Yee node. The resolved skin-depth interior never needs meshing (validated to <5% of the analytic reflection at >=10x fewer cells than a resolved volumetric metal). Analytic helpers `surface_impedance(omega)` / `surface_impedance_at_freq(f)` / `skin_depth(f)` remain exposed for design
- The `LossyMetalMedium` surface-impedance boundary is generalized from axis-aligned metal `Box` faces to any **staircased (voxelized) good conductor**: a non-`Box` conductor (a curved cylinder/sphere, all six exposed-face orientations, mixed orientations in one scene) is staircased from its node occupancy into masked per-face Leontovich writes, with each exposed voxel face illuminated only when its vacuum-side node lies inside the physical domain. Edges and corners are handled by the standard per-face-independent surface update (no new edge physics). This is validated at two levels beyond the flat-plate analytic reflection: (1) a **wave-level skin-effect attenuation benchmark** (`benchmark/scenes/rf/lossy_waveguide_attenuation.py`, RESULTS row `rf/lossy_waveguide_attenuation`) where a lossy-wall TE10 rectangular waveguide's conductor attenuation `alpha`, extracted from the two-line `|S21|` ratio of a short and a long guide, tracks the analytic TE10 `alpha_c` (Pozar 3.96) to a fraction of a percent across the band while a PEC-wall guide of the same geometry gives `alpha ~ 0`; and (2) a **resolved-conductor physics gate** (`tests/validation/physics/test_sibc_cylinder_convergence.py`) where the power absorbed by a staircased lossy-metal cylinder (skin depth unmeshed) reproduces the power absorbed by the same cylinder as a fully-resolved volumetric conductor (skin depth meshed on a grid-converged fine reference) within a documented tolerance, an order of magnitude closer than a PEC cylinder. True oblique/conformal (non-staircase) SIBC, a rotated metal `Box`, a generic rational `SurfaceImpedanceMedium` on a curved conductor, and Bloch + SIBC remain fail-closed with a physical reason
- Validation note (no new adapter capability): the pre-existing `LossyMetalMedium` surface export through `Scene.to_tidy3d()` (see the reference-backend adapter section) was exercised as an external reference for the lossy-waveguide attenuation benchmark. No adapter code changed; the forward-mode attenuation cross-check is recorded honestly (the external RF surface-impedance export under-applies the wall loss at a coarse export grid, a documented adapter-fidelity gap, while the analytic `alpha_c` and the FDTD two-line `alpha` remain the binding references)
- Passive dispersion fitting through `fit_dispersion(frequencies, permittivity, num_poles=3, ...)` (and `fit_dispersion_nk(wavelengths, n, k)` for complex-index tables, `permittivity_from_nk`, and `load_dispersion_csv(path, abscissa="wavelength"|"frequency", unit=..., quantity="nk"|"permittivity")` for two/three-column text tables): tabulated complex permittivity becomes an ordinary `Material` carrying `LorentzPole` / `DrudePole` / `DebyePole` terms (plus an optional static `sigma_e`), returned as `DispersionFit(material, report)`. Pole identification reuses the shared vector fitter (`fit_rational`) at every order up to `2*num_poles`; the poles are projected onto passive Lorentz/Drude/Debye families by non-negative least squares (passive by construction: positive strengths, non-negative damping), jointly refined with L-BFGS in torch `float64`, pruned of negligible terms and of in-band resonances narrower than the sample spacing, and the most parsimonious order within 5% of the best error is kept. Options cover the term budget, a frequency window, per-sample weights (default relative-error weighting), fixed or bounded `eps_inf` (default `eps_inf >= 1`), the allowed pole families, and an error tolerance. `DispersionFitReport` (a `FitReport`) records in-band RMS/max absolute and relative errors, the sampled passivity margin `min Im(eps)` over a band extended 100x beyond the fit window on both sides, the s-domain poles and their stability, `eps_inf`, `sigma_e` and the per-family term counts. Validated on CPU by exact recovery of synthetic Lorentz, Drude, Debye and mixed Drude+Lorentz parameters, passive fits of 2%-noise data that track the clean model between samples, (n, k) and CSV round trips, and few-pole fits of the library silica and gold models
- Literature material library `MATERIAL_LIBRARY` / `library_material(key)`: `MaterialLibraryEntry` records with the exact citation, DOI, model kind and valid vacuum-wavelength range, each building an ordinary `Material` through `Material.sellmeier(...)` (coefficients converted from `micron^2` to SI `meter^2`) or explicit `DrudePole` / `LorentzPole` lists. Entries: fused silica `SiO2` (Malitson 1965 Sellmeier, 0.21-3.71 um), `Si3N4` (Luke et al. 2015 Sellmeier, 0.31-5.504 um), sapphire `Al2O3` ordinary ray (Malitson 1962 Sellmeier, 0.2652-5.577 um), crystalline silicon `cSi` in its transparent band (Salzberg-Villa 1957 data, Tatian 1984 Sellmeier, 1.357-11.04 um), and the metals `Ag`, `Au`, `Cu`, `Al` (Rakic et al. 1998 Lorentz-Drude model, with eV parameters converted exactly). Checked against published reference indices (for example silica n = 1.4440 at 1.55 um, sapphire n_o = 1.7681 at 589.3 nm) and, when the external reference package is installed, cross-checked against its same-literature variants (closed-form variants agree to 1e-8 in permittivity)
- In-domain perfect-magnetic-conductor material via `Material.pmc(name=None)` / `Material.is_pmc`, the electromagnetic dual of `Material.pec()`: PMC structures compile to a union PMC node occupancy (never a bulk dielectric) and the FDTD magnetic update holds every H component whose four-node dual-edge occupancy average is >= 0.5 at zero (staircase, folded into the H decay/curl coefficients; the wall sits on the first dual plane inside the declared face). FDFD zeroes the magnetic-flux weight of the same H dual edges in the curl-curl operator. Validated on CUDA by a plane-wave slab whose reflection is `|r| = 1` with `r_PMC = -r_PEC`, vanishing tangential H on the PMC wall with a doubled tangential E, agreement with a volumetric good magnetic conductor (`sigma_m`) to 1e-3, and FDFD-vs-FDTD agreement. PMC requires volumetric geometry (no PlanarPEC/SurfaceMesh), carries no other constitutive channel, has no trainable-geometry gradient channel (rejected by the FDTD adjoint), cannot be crossed by a mode-solver plane, and FDFD H fields reconstructed from curl E strictly inside the PMC volume are not masked. The reference adapter exports it as the dedicated PMC medium
- `Material.custom_sellmeier(b_coefficients, c_coefficients, eps_inf=1.0, ...)` for spatially varying lossless Sellmeier dielectrics: each `B_i` is a non-negative 3D torch grid (or scalar) and each `C_i` [m^2] a positive scalar or 3D grid, rasterized over the owning structure's `Box` with the `CustomPole` convention. Every term lowers to zero-damping `CustomLorentzPole` entries with the `Material.sellmeier` mapping, so uniform coefficients compile to exactly the same pole parameters and weights as `Material.sellmeier`; because ADE recursion constants are scalar, a spatially varying `C_i` must be piecewise constant (one pole per distinct level, at most 16 per term) and a continuously varying resonance is rejected. Coefficient grids are fixed inputs (`requires_grad` grids are rejected: per-cell pole strengths have no solver gradient channel). The reference adapter exports spatially uniform coefficients through the ordinary Lorentz path (validated against the reference custom-Sellmeier `eps_model`) and rejects varying ones like other custom poles
- `PerturbationMedium(..., debye_sensitivities=(...), drude_sensitivities=(...), lorentz_sensitivities=(...))` perturbs the base material's electric pole strengths with the same perturbation field (pole-residue perturbation of a temperature- or carrier-dependent dispersive medium): `delta_eps_i(x) = delta_eps_i + s_i * perturbation(x)` for Debye/Lorentz and `plasma_frequency_i(x) = plasma_frequency_i + s_i * perturbation(x)` for Drude, one sensitivity per base pole. Perturbed poles lower to the matching `CustomPole` and compile through the existing custom-pole rasterization and ADE kernels; zero perturbation reproduces the base material's compiled coefficients exactly and a uniform perturbation equals the directly constructed shifted material. Pole rate parameters (`tau`, `gamma`, `resonance_frequency`) are not perturbable (scalar ADE constants), gain Lorentz poles are rejected, and a trainable perturbation with any nonzero pole sensitivity is rejected (no pole-strength gradient channel; the `eps_sensitivity` channel stays differentiable). The reference adapter exports a spatially uniform perturbation as the shifted homogeneous medium (validated against the reference perturbation pole-residue medium)
- Opt-in conductor surface roughness on `LossyMetalMedium(conductivity=..., roughness=...)` through frozen `HammerstadRoughness(rms_height, roughness_factor=2.0)`, `HurayRoughness(sphere_radius, sphere_density, relative_area=1.0)` (plus `HurayRoughness.from_surface_ratio(...)` and `HurayRoughness.cannonball(radius)`) and `GroisseRoughness(rms_height)`, all exported from `witwin.maxwell` (`witwin/maxwell/roughness.py`). Each model multiplies the smooth Leontovich impedance by a skin-depth-dependent factor `K`; Hammerstad and Huray use their causal complex forms, whose resistance ratio `Re(K * (1 - i))` equals the classic Hammerstad `1 + (RF-1)(2/pi)atan(1.4 (Rq/delta)^2)` and Huray `A_matte/A_flat + 1.5 f / (1 + delta/r + delta^2/(2 r^2))` loss formulas exactly while adding the matching internal reactance; Groisse is a real, non-causal factor. The narrowband FDTD surface scales its resistance by the loss factor at the source frequency (Box and staircased faces); `LossyMetalMedium.rational_surface_impedance(frequency_range, order=6)` fits the rough impedance (roughness applied before fitting) as a passive-certified, accuracy-gated `RationalSurfaceImpedance` for `SurfaceImpedanceMedium` (Groisse is rejected there). `roughness=None` keeps every smooth path bit-identical. Hammerstad and Huray export through the reference-backend adapter; Groisse export is rejected.
- Broadband rough-surface guard: `LossyMetalMedium.rational_surface_impedance(...)` with roughness raises `ValueError` and points to the narrowband surface when the time-stepping Z-form refit's direct (high-frequency) impedance exceeds 3.1x the smooth conductor's, the validated envelope (stable: Hammerstad RF=2 ratio 2.01, RF=3 ratio 3.04; diverged: cannonball Huray ratios 5.97 and 7.53). The 3.1 bound is empirical, calibrated at 50 S/m, 0.5-5 GHz, order 6, 40-60 cells/wavelength; it is not a stability proof, since the divergence is resolution-dependent.
- Known limitation (pre-existing): the generic broadband surface-impedance update over-predicts absorption once `|Z_s|` is large, independent of roughness (a smooth 15 S/m conductor reads about +12% at 45-60 cells/wavelength); the narrowband surface reproduces the analytic absorption within 1%.

## Sources

- `CW`, `GaussianPulse`, and `RickerWavelet` source-time definitions
- FDTD source timing: additive electric currents (`PointDipole`, `UniformCurrentSource`, electric `CustomCurrentSource`) sample `(n+1/2)*dt` for the `H^(n+1/2) -> E^(n+1)` update; magnetic currents sample `n*dt`. Source normalisation samples the same injection instant, including the half-step lumped/coax port drive. Combined equivalent J/M sources (`CustomFieldSource`, TFSF, beams and mode sources) retain a single incident-envelope reference at `n*dt`. Observer DFT time origins are unchanged. Compared with results produced before this change, source-normalised port quantities (`port_phasors`, normalised `port_voltage`/current and `Result.port_drive`-relative values, lumped and coaxial) rotate by `+omega*dt/2` in the harmonic limit, while raw port V/I, S-parameters, impedance `Z = V/I` and reflection coefficients are unchanged; raw additive-J field outputs rotate by `-omega*dt/2`. Finite records use the actual sampled envelope.
- `BroadbandPulse(freq_range, minimum_amplitude=0.3, amplitude, phase, offset)` source time with significant energy over a whole frequency range: a band-centred Gaussian-envelope carrier minus a slow Gaussian pedestal that removes its DC content exactly (zero-mean drive, so fields decay), with `fwidth` solved as the narrowest spectral width whose zero-phase amplitude spectrum stays at or above `minimum_amplitude` (`0.05 < m < 0.5`) of its in-band peak across the band (tight at the band edges; reduces to the band-centred `GaussianPulse` for narrow bands). Exposes `frequency`, `fwidth`, `sigma_t`, `delay` (automatic delay plus `offset` in units of `1/(2*pi*bandwidth)`), `characteristic_frequency`, `settling_time`, and the exact analytic `spectrum(frequencies)`; it is evaluated analytically by the native time-shifted surface kernel, the Bloch/adjoint torch paths and the scalar point/volume path
- `CustomSourceTime` arbitrary temporal waveform from a sampled `(times, amplitudes)` table (uniform or non-uniform samples, `interpolation="linear"` (default) or natural `"cubic"` spline, zero outside the sampled range) or a callable `fn`, with an optional reference `frequency` (launch dispersion tuning, beam profiles and mode solves on surface sources; defaults to `characteristic_frequency`). Point and volume current sources evaluate it on the solver time grid; `PlaneWave`, `GaussianBeam`, `AstigmaticGaussianBeam`, `ModeSource`, TFSF beams and `CustomFieldSource` evaluate a table natively on the GPU at every cell's delayed time (the table is uploaded once; uniform tables use O(1) segment lookup, non-uniform ones a binary search), and a cubic table sampled from a `GaussianPulse` reproduces the analytic pulse-driven fields to about `1e-6` relative; the FDTD adjoint replays the same table at the same per-cell delays
- `PointDipole` source definition with `source_time` and selectable `profile="gaussian"|"ideal"`
- `PointDipole`, `ModeSource`, `ModeMonitor`, and `ModePort` use `position=...` as the public spatial-location argument
- `PointDipole` Gaussian profiles are normalized by the local Yee control volumes so their integrated SI current moment is invariant to width and grid spacing
- Gaussian point dipoles narrower than about 3 mm now inject their full unit moment; previously a units error in the normalization floor under-injected them, so absolute fields and powers of such scenes increase
- `PointDipole(profile="ideal")` deposits the same unit current moment onto the neighboring Yee samples with linear position-preserving weights
- FDTD `PointDipole` profiles that reach a periodic or Bloch face are folded onto the unique in-domain Yee samples as the periodic sum of their images (Floquet phase `exp(-i k n L)` per wrapped image on Bloch axes), so every component compiles to one source patch (two for a complex Bloch profile) regardless of the period, keeps unit current moment, and an `ideal` delta lands on its periodic representative instead of being clamped onto the boundary cell; dipoles whose cutoff window stays inside the period compile exactly as before
- `PlaneWave` soft source for analytical plane-wave injection on an auto-placed source plane; zero-phase periodic boundaries are supported on transverse axes, while periodicity along the propagation direction, a nonzero tangential phase advance, and Bloch boundaries require the TFSF path
- Soft plane-wave/Gaussian-beam sheets no longer write into non-updated outer-wall E nodes, removing late-time energy growth at transverse PML corners (interior fields change by ~1e-3).
- `PlaneWave` soft source uses a single-plane directional `E/H` equivalent-current injector whose absolute incident-power scale is derived from first principles (surface-equivalence unit forward gain and the Yee numerical wave impedance `eta0`): unit source amplitude radiates unit time-averaged power over the full computational aperture, including external PML, with the analytic `1/sqrt(A*cos(theta)/(2*eta0))` scale and the derived Yee half-cell Poynting factor `1/sqrt(cos(k_normal*dl_normal/2))`; there is no empirical calibration factor, and absolute native-monitor power matches analytic within 2% across frequencies and grid spacings
- The soft `PlaneWave` numerical-dispersion phase correction is local to the launch footprint on nonuniform (graded) grids: the discrete-dispersion phase velocity that time-aligns the Yee `E`/`H` source planes (and phases the aperture for oblique incidence) is solved from the launch-plane cell spacing along the injection axis and the physical-aperture mean spacing along each tangential axis, instead of the global-minimum spacing whose finest cell can sit far from the source. On a grid whose interior is uniform along an axis, that axis returns its exact spacing, so the correction is bit-for-bit the previous global-minimum result and uniform grids are unchanged; on a ~2.6x-graded propagation axis with the source in the coarse region (~6 cells/wavelength there) the injected wavefront's phase-velocity error drops from ~4% to 0 (an exact match to the launch cell's own numerical wavenumber). Soft plane-wave, Gaussian-beam, and astigmatic-beam sheets launch the prescribed positive incident E and matching H; the common field-sign correction preserves power and the existing observer clock convention.
- `GaussianBeam` soft source for analytical Gaussian-beam injection with configurable waist and focus
- `AstigmaticGaussianBeam` soft source for elliptical Gaussian-beam injection with independent per-axis waist `(w0_u, w0_v)` and per-axis focal offsets `focus_u`/`focus_v`, reducing exactly to `GaussianBeam` for isotropic inputs, normalized to one watt, and using the launch plane as the common pulsed-time origin so a remote waist does not truncate the pulse before `t=0`
- `UniformCurrentSource(size, polarization, source_time, center=...)` for a uniform additive electric current filling an axis-aligned box region, deposited by the geometric overlap of the source box with each Yee component's dual control volume
- `CustomCurrentSource(current_dataset, source_time)` injects volume electric-current (`Jx/Jy/Jz`, A/m²) and magnetic-current (`Mx/My/Mz`, V/m²) densities over the endpoint-defined dataset volume, clipped to the physical domain. Component-aware Yee control cells receive fractional volume coverage and in-span trilinear samples; uncovered cells receive no current. Singleton dataset axes retain the nearest corresponding node- or half-step Yee plane rule
- `CustomFieldSource(field_dataset, source_time)` discretizes the independent equivalent currents `J = n x H`, `M = -n x E` on the declared physical plane. Fractional Yee control-cell overlaps preserve the dataset aperture; interpolation stays inside its sample span. Two-way modal weights distribute each current onto its bracketing normal Yee sheets, removing artificial half-cell gain and backward radiation for compatible plane-wave inputs while retaining the physical two-sided radiation of partial or incompatible inputs. Transverse continuation respects wall parities and full-span periodic/Bloch phases
- Experimental `ModeSource` soft source for axis-aligned FDTD waveguide launching, using a full-vector generalized 2D eigenmode solve with adjoint forward/backward Yee differences on structured apertures to prevent odd/even checkerboard copies, a centered TE/TM parity basis for uniform metallic guides, deterministic sparse initialization and smoothness tie-breaking, deterministic requested-polarization rotation inside numerically degenerate eigenspaces, power-inner-product duplicate rejection, and requested-polarization-family ordering for stable physical `mode_index` selection. For a guided (non-TEM) mode request on a closed metallic aperture, a transverse null-space branch at `beta = k0` (a plane-wave-like profile with no transverse cutoff) is rejected structurally -- by a block-averaged, anti-checkerboard transverse-envelope test combined with `beta^2 ~ k0^2` -- so the guided mode is returned at every grid tier instead of the spurious `beta = k0` eigenvalue; the rejection is gated on the requested wave family, so `beta = k0` for a doubly-connected TEM line (solved on the separate electrostatic path) is unchanged. Full-plane component profiles retain zero-valued boundary nodes and use discrete one-watt Poynting normalization on transverse Yee planes, normal half-cell power compensation on resolved grids, and leapfrog-aligned propagation delays across staggered E/H launch faces in forward, replay, and adjoint execution. Dense and sparse forward backends coexist with the retained torch-differentiable scalar path for trainable scenes, while modal results expose per-candidate eigenpair/divergence/power/polarization/checkerboard diagnostics and the normalized power-overlap matrix
- Experimental `ModeSource` / `ModeMonitor` / `ModePort` now accept broadband (`GaussianPulse` / `Ricker`) as well as `CW` soft injection (the guided profile is solved once at the waveform center frequency and driven by the native time-shifted surface kernel), complex/lossy eigenmodes (a complex-symmetric scalar solve exposing `effective_index_complex` / `beta_complex`, matched to `~4e-5` against the analytic lossy-slab dispersion), diagonal-anisotropic apertures solved with their true per-axis `eps`/`mu` tensors instead of an isotropic average, and bent (curved-waveguide) ports via the Heiblum-Harris conformal map (`bend_radius` / `bend_axis`, threaded through `mode_spec` so the S-parameter / mode-overlap postprocess reconstructs the same bent reference mode)
- Raw TFSF incident timing is physically exact on the Yee clock: auxiliary entry E uses W((n+1)dt), and analytic/discrete CW faces retain their physical J/M instants; normalization references follow each provider.
- Pulsed TFSF incident lines terminate with a qualified absorber appended beyond the sampled extent; old checkpoints with such lines are refused.
- Experimental `TFSF(bounds=...)` injection descriptor for `PlaneWave`, `GaussianBeam`, and `AstigmaticGaussianBeam`, with validated axis-aligned `PlaneWave` support for `CW` and `GaussianPulse`, and validated CW oblique `PlaneWave` support
- Experimental `TFSF.slab(axis="x"|"y"|"z", bounds=...)` injection descriptor for grating-oriented `PlaneWave` workflows on any normal axis (both CW and broadband `GaussianPulse` / `RickerWavelet` source times), spanning the transverse Bloch unit cell (the two axes transverse to the chosen normal axis) during solver preparation for periodic grating illumination; the two slab faces normal to `axis` are selected automatically and the oblique polarization is resolved from the incident direction, so the forward stepping, mixed Bloch/CPML update, and reverse-time grating adjoint all dispatch on the resolved single PML (normal) axis rather than assuming z. A pulsed source injects its per-cell time-delayed surface currents into the split real/imag Bloch field with the boundary wrap phase, so a single broadband run covers the band at a fixed transverse Bloch wavevector (automatic wavevector resolution still requires CW because it maps a single frequency to a wavevector)
- Non-periodic (PML/absorbing transverse boundary) `TFSF.slab` injection for a normally-incident `PlaneWave` (`CW` or pulsed): the two-face slab fills the whole transverse cross section, injecting a plane wave that propagates along the slab axis with the scattered-field region confined outside the two axis-normal faces (the standard 1D-style reflection/transmission setup through a layered stack). It reuses the axis-aligned auxiliary-line provider, so the forward and adjoint runtimes need no slab-specific branch; oblique incidence under non-periodic transverse boundaries is rejected with a physics-worded error (use Bloch transverse boundaries for the grating slab)
- CUDA `PlaneWave` TFSF forward stepping uses native CUDA auxiliary-line updates and fused patch-application kernels to reduce per-step launch overhead
- Pulsed auxiliary-line `PlaneWave` TFSF defines the declared waveform (amplitude, phase, and time origin) at the physical upstream Yee plane of the injection box, or its entry supporting plane for oblique incidence, independently of PML padding. Discrete Yee continuation supplies the upstream staggered samples without a dispersive inverse; propagation into the box stays causal. CW and Bloch analytic slab references are unchanged. Resume checkpoints record this convention and refuse older pulsed auxiliary-line states before fingerprint comparison. Resume restores the auxiliary clock from the saved step; adjoint forward and replay stage the same boundary samples, with native auxiliary arithmetic retained during detached replay.
- `GaussianBeam` `TFSF` remains experimental and currently uses the analytical profile provider rather than the future angular-spectrum / discrete-face engine
- Polarization specified by field name (`"Ex"`, `"Ey"`, `"Ez"`) or explicit 3-vector
- Source amplitude and phase carried by `source_time`; spatial sources keep width / beam parameters and optional name
- Multiple sources per scene
- Source compilation uses `compile_fdfd_sources(...)` / `compile_fdtd_sources(...)` list-based interfaces across both solvers

## Grating TFSF Example

```python
import witwin.maxwell as mw

scene = mw.Scene(
    domain=mw.Domain(bounds=((-0.5, 0.5), (-0.5, 0.5), (-1.0, 1.0))),
    grid=mw.GridSpec.uniform(0.05),
    boundary=mw.BoundarySpec.faces(
        default="pml",
        num_layers=12,
        strength=1.0,
        x="bloch",
        y="bloch",
        z="pml",
        bloch_wavevector="auto",
    ),
)

scene.add_source(
    mw.PlaneWave(
        direction=(0.25, 0.0, 0.9682458366),
        polarization=(1.0, 0.0, -0.2581988897),
        source_time=mw.CW(frequency=200e12),
        injection=mw.TFSF.slab(axis="z", bounds=(-0.4, 0.4)),
    )
)

scene.add_monitor(mw.FluxMonitor("transmission", axis="z", position=0.7))

result = mw.Simulation.fdtd(scene, frequencies=[200e12]).run()
```

## Monitors

- `PointMonitor` for point sampling of selected electric or magnetic field components
- `postprocess.plane_payload(...)` builds native unnormalized plane, finite-plane (including sampled and closed-surface faces), and flux payloads from external complex Yee fields, sharing FDTD observer geometry and assembly for `Result.from_fields(...)`.
- `PlaneMonitor` for orthogonal plane sampling of selected electric or magnetic field components
- `FinitePlaneMonitor` for first-class finite rectangular plane sampling with explicit `position=(x, y, z)` and `size=(sx, sy, sz)` on a zero-thickness axis
- `ClosedSurfaceMonitor` for first-class finite closed Huygens-surface workflows, including `ClosedSurfaceMonitor.box(...)` and custom multi-face axis-aligned surfaces built from `FinitePlaneMonitor` faces
- Closed surfaces are watertight on the grid: every `ClosedSurfaceMonitor` face and every box projection-monitor face (open boxes included) is placed on its nearest tangential-E Yee node, and its in-plane edges are the snapped planes of the adjacent faces (`FinitePlaneMonitor.closure_planes`), for native and sampled quadrature and on x-slab shards. A declared off-node box therefore closes exactly. In vacuum with an exterior source, the native net flux is zero to float32 accumulation, and the absorbed power of an enclosed lossy body does not depend on where the box bounds fall within a cell and matches its `PowerLossMonitor` conduction loss. Face payloads report the snapped `tangential_bounds` next to `declared_tangential_bounds`, and NF2FF places the equivalent currents at the snapped `yee_plane_position`. A face that snaps exactly onto a CopperFoil sheet keeps the existing explicit-side refusal
- `FluxMonitor` for plane-integrated power / flux extraction from tangential `E/H` fields; spectral flux planes snap to the nearest tangential-E Yee plane, symmetrically average the straddling H planes, crop external-PML samples back to the physical `Domain.bounds`, and integrate cell-centred transverse samples with full control-volume widths (including endpoint cells), avoiding arbitrary-position phasor attenuation and one-cell aperture undercounting
- Spectral point / plane / flux / closed-surface monitors accumulate the running-DFT with the physical Yee time stagger: electric observers sample at the plain step phase while magnetic observers carry an extra `-1/2` step (`-0.5*omega*dt`) retard, so the time-averaged Poynting cross term `S = 1/2 Re(E x H*)` sees the correct `+1/2`-step E/H offset; the FDTD adjoint seed schedule applies the exact transpose of this per-field phase
- Near-to-far-field (Stratton-Chu / NF2FF) surface quadrature integrates each equivalent-current sample over its full primal Yee control volume (cell-centred midpoint rule), so on a closed Huygens box the control volumes tile the box surface to machine precision and the radiated far field is box-size independent (surface-equivalence invariant), giving correct directional far fields, RCS, and closed-surface radiated power
- `FieldTimeMonitor` for raw time-domain FDTD field recording (point, plane, or volume region) with `start` / `stop` / `interval` sampling controls, returning the sample-time tensor `t` and per-component GPU buffers through `Result.monitor(...)`
- `FluxTimeMonitor` for instantaneous time-domain Poynting flux (`E x H`) recorded on an axis-aligned plane with `start` / `stop` / `interval` sampling controls, returning the sample-time tensor `t` and the flux time series through `Result.monitor(...)`; external-PML transverse samples receive zero integration weight so the default infinite plane remains the physical-domain aperture
- Experimental `ModeMonitor` for first-class modal decomposition on an axis-aligned port plane, reusing the current `ModeSource` mode specification and returning forward / backward modal amplitudes and power through `Result.monitor(...)`
- Experimental `DiffractionMonitor` for grating diffraction-order decomposition on an axis-aligned periodic unit-cell plane, returning per-order `(m, n)` complex tangential amplitudes, per-order Poynting power, propagating / evanescent flags, and diffraction angles through `Result.monitor(...)`, with reciprocal lattice and light-cone cutoff derived from the transverse periods and the resolved Bloch wavevector
- `PermittivityMonitor` and `MediumMonitor` for first-class point / plane / volume sampling of the compiled material properties (`eps`, plus `mu` and `sigma_e` for `MediumMonitor`), resolved from the compiled material tensors at result time through `Result.monitor(...)` without requesting any time stepping or extra DFT frequencies
- Experimental `DipoleEmissionMonitor` for the power a named `PointDipole` delivers to the field (`P = -(1/2) Re(conj(J) . E)`), formed from the co-located electric-field DFT at the dipole cell and the injected source current spectrum, returning per-frequency `power_delivered` through `Result.monitor(...)`; combine a structured run with a vacuum run through `maxwell.postprocess.purcell_factor(...)` to obtain the Purcell factor / local density of states
- Optional per-monitor `frequencies=` on plane and modal monitors / ports
- Named monitor results returned through the unified `Result` object
- Multi-component plane monitors with aggregated `Result.monitor(...)` payloads and collocated tangential grids for postprocessing workflows
- `Scene.resolved_monitors()` expands `ClosedSurfaceMonitor` into its underlying finite face monitors while preserving the public `Scene -> Simulation -> Result` workflow
- Multi-frequency monitor output with `Result.monitor(name, frequency=...)` / `freq_index=...` selection
- Optional `compute_flux=True` on `PlaneMonitor` to emit integrated Poynting flux / power per frequency
- `Result.raw_monitor(...)` to access the underlying point / plane payload directly when a first-class modal monitor resolves to a higher-level modal result
- Declarative field-projection monitors `AngleProjectionMonitor` (`theta` x `phi` at `radius`), `CartesianProjectionMonitor` (local `x` x `y` on a plane normal to `axis` at `distance`) and `KSpaceProjectionMonitor` (direction cosines `ux` x `uy` about `axis`, evanescent samples masked): the Huygens surface is a plane patch (one zero extent, `normal_direction`) or a box whose outward faces can be dropped with `exclude_surfaces=("z_neg", ...)`; the run records the tangential running-DFT fields on the faces (`<name>::<face>`), and `Result.monitor(name)` returns the same `ProjectedFieldData` (`[F, *grid, 3]` E/H, spherical components, power density) that `FieldProjector` produces for that surface, with the origin defaulting to the surface center, optional planar `window_size`, homogeneous-exterior medium detection, `frequency=` / `freq_index=` selection, and autograd-differentiable FDTD adjoint objectives; `Result.raw_monitor(name)` exposes the recorded closed-surface payload
- `GaussianOverlapMonitor` and `AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitor` decompose the fields on a finite plane into a paraxial Gaussian beam declared with the `GaussianBeam` / `AstigmaticGaussianBeam` vocabulary (`direction`, `polarization`, `beam_waist`, `focus`, plus `focus_u` / `focus_v`), evaluated in the medium at the monitor center; `Result.monitor(name)` returns per-frequency forward / backward amplitudes in `sqrt(W)` against the unit-power profile (backward = tangentially mirrored profile, as for `ModeMonitor`), the matching powers, net plane flux and power fractions, differentiable in FDTD adjoint objectives
- `SurfaceFieldTimeMonitor` records time-domain Yee fields on a plane patch or an (open) box surface with `start` / `stop` / `interval` step controls; each face copies every requested component from its nearest native Yee plane over the face extent, returned through `Result.monitor(name)["faces"][label]` with per-component coordinates and the E/H half-step time offsets
- `AuxFieldTimeMonitor` records time-domain auxiliary dispersive-material state at a point, patch or volume: electric polarization `Px/Py/Pz` (Debye/Lorentz poles), polarization current density `Jpx/Jpy/Jpz` (all electric poles), and the magnetic-pole counterparts `Mx/My/Mz`, `Jmx/Jmy/Jmz`, summed over poles; absent state records zeros and is flagged in `present`. Instantaneous Kerr / chi2 / two-photon-absorption media keep no auxiliary state and free-carrier densities are not modeled
- `SurfaceFieldTimeMonitor` and `AuxFieldTimeMonitor` traces are detached; in a differentiable FDTD run any objective that depends on them raises a clear error during `backward()`. All four monitor families are FDTD-only and single-GPU for the time traces

## Ports

- Experimental `ModePort` scene object that declaratively couples an optional `ModeSource` excitation with a first-class `ModeMonitor`, so modal ports still flow through the same `Scene -> Simulation -> Result` public architecture
- `ModePort(source_time=...)` materializes a named `ModeSource` plus a named modal monitor; `ModePort(source_time=None)` acts as a monitor-only modal port
- `ModePort(monitor_offset=...)` can separate the launch plane from the sampled monitor plane along the port normal without introducing a second public solver entrypoint
- Experimental RF `WavePort` / `WaveModeSpec` declarations freeze an axis-aligned aperture, explicit propagation direction and coincident reference plane, deterministic per-port mode identities, a tangential polarization seed, and peak-phasor impedance definitions. `Scene.compile_waveports()` snaps the aperture and optional TEM/hybrid voltage path and current contour to one Yee grid. `Simulation.fdtd(...)` supports both a named single-mode `PortExcitation(..., mode_name=...)` and independent-column `PortSweep`; the latter returns a complete multimode `NetworkData`, while both return modal `PortData` with explicit `[M, F]` propagation constants, characteristic impedances, and tracking confidence. Cross-frequency assignment combines propagation constants and modal overlap, including degenerate-subspace alignment. Uniform conductor-backed TEM apertures use a device-resident electrostatic mode solve and one-watt power-consistent V/I normalization; TE uses `Z=omega*mu/beta`, TM uses `Z=beta/(omega*epsilon)`, and hybrid modes explicitly select voltage/current or power normalization. WavePort mode solves and FDTD columns execute sequentially on one CUDA device
- Experimental `ModalPortSweep(ports=..., modes=..., run_only=..., element_mappings=..., source_time=...)` passed as `Simulation.fdtd(scene, ..., excitations=sweep).run()` (or `maxwell.run_modal_port_sweep(Simulation.fdtd(...), sweep)`) builds the full N-port, multi-mode scattering matrix of a photonic component from its `ModePort`s: one ordinary FDTD run per selected `(port, mode)` input on a cloned scene with every other `ModePort` monitor-only, modal projection of every port plane onto unit-power reference modes (any mode index in the port polarization family or an explicit `(polarization, index)` pair), and normalization by the measured incident wave of the driven mode. The `Result.network` `NetworkData` uses deterministic `[frequency, out, in]` order with `"<port>::E<axis><index>"` channel labels, nominal `z0 = 1` modal power waves in sqrt(W), reference planes at each `ModePort.position` (the `monitor_offset` is de-embedded with the modal propagation constant, stored in `metadata["propagation_constants"]` for `shift_reference_planes`), and a `passive_incident_ratio` diagnostic; `Result.ports` carries per-port modal `PortData`. `run_only` drives a column subset, ordered `element_mappings` `((out, in), (out, in), c)` fill symmetric entries without extra runs, and columns that stay incomplete are reported through `NetworkData.valid_columns` with zero placeholders. Columns execute sequentially on one CUDA device; non-FDTD simulations reject the sweep, and the sweep executes through `Simulation.run()` (each column is its own FDTD run)
- Experimental `CoaxialLumpedPort(name, center=..., inner_diameter=..., outer_diameter=..., normal_axis=..., direction="+", reference_impedance=50, termination=SeriesRLC | None)` declares an annular lumped port on a primal Yee node plane between two concentric PEC conductors. At FDTD preparation the in-plane edges left unsuppressed by PEC between the two discrete conductors are solved as a discrete-harmonic resistive sheet (potential 1 on the inner and 0 on the outer conductor); the normalized edge currents form one two-component power-dual voltage row, so Kirchhoff's law holds at every vacuum node (no trapped DC node charge) and the row equals the inner-to-outer voltage of any in-plane curl-free field. One scalar implicit-midpoint series branch (Thevenin source or SeriesRLC load) couples it with the same update order as the rectangular lumped runtime (bitwise identical for a one-component row). Driven/terminated ports report the branch current entering the field; passive observer ports report the staircase H circulation around the inner conductor on the `direction` side. Works with `PortExcitation` and `PortSweep` (`NetworkData`). CUDA gates (no fitted line parameter): the reference characteristic impedance is that of the staircased line, `eta0 / C~` with `C~` the lattice capacitance of the compiled conductor-node sets (56.910 ohm for the round coax at 0.25 mm, converging at first order to the nominal 49.94 ohm; 28.448 ohm for the square coax). On the round coax the port launches a TEM wave at c (plane-monitor phase fit within 0.1 %, measured 0.9999-1.0000), two passive coaxial probes measure that impedance within 1e-3 (measured 6e-6) and beta = k0 within 1e-4 at 0.25 mm and track it at 0.125 mm, the open conductor ends are lossless constant shunt capacitances, the resistor adds exactly `1/R` (within 1e-3), and 25/50/100 ohm loads and a 50 ohm two-port sweep match ideal line theory with those end capacitances within 1e-3 (measured <= 3.4e-5); the 50 ohm termination of the 56.9 ohm line therefore reads `|S11|` 0.023 / 0.048 / 0.075 at 1 / 2 / 3 GHz. The square coax passes the same velocity (within 0.2 %), probe and load gates. Forward-only: trainable scenes, dispersive row support and circuit/embedded-network binding are rejected
- Experimental `CoaxialLumpedResistor(name, center=..., inner_diameter=..., outer_diameter=..., normal_axis=..., resistance=...)` is the same annular branch as a standalone passive scene lumped element (`Scene.add_lumped_element`); it is bitwise identical to a `CoaxialLumpedPort` terminated by `SeriesRLC(r=resistance)` at the same plane, and its state is part of the lumped checkpoint schema
- `PECFrame(length=2).structures(port, scene)` materializes the reference PEC injection frame of a `WavePort`, `ModePort` or `ModeSource`: four named zero-thickness `PlanarPEC` side walls on the snapped aperture rectangle, `length` primal cells from the aperture plane toward the injection direction, tagged `pec_frame` for adapter export. It uses the existing hard tangential PlanarPEC ownership; CUDA checks confirm that only the wall edges are shorted and that the shielded coax thru `S21` changes by less than 1e-3
- Lumped, terminal and coaxial port excitations accept any sampled-table `CustomSourceTime`, `BroadbandPulse`, and the new DC-containing `BasebandGaussianPulse(twidth, offset=5, amplitude=1)` / `BasebandRectangularPulse(rise_time, twidth, offset=5, amplitude=1)` (natural-cubic tables, so they also drive every other table-capable source) for TDR-like transient runs. The drive is evaluated on device every step through the shared table/broadband kernel; existing CW/Gaussian/Ricker port waveforms are unchanged. On a matched coax the S11 from table, broadband and baseband drives equals the analytic Gaussian drive within 1e-4 / 2e-3 / 2e-3, and the baseband drive also resolves a 0.1 GHz sample. Callable `CustomSourceTime(fn)` port drives and complex table amplitudes are rejected
- `LowFrequencySmoothingSpec(min_sampling_time=1, max_sampling_time=5, order=1, max_deviation=0.5)` with `smooth_low_frequency(data, spec, run_time=...)` post-processes `NetworkData`, `PortData` or raw spectra: samples recorded for fewer than `min_sampling_time` periods of the run window are replaced by a complex least-squares polynomial of `order` fitted to the samples between `min_sampling_time` and `max_sampling_time` periods, optionally pulled back to within `max_deviation` of the lowest trusted sample; differentiable, device-resident, and recorded in `metadata["low_frequency_smoothing"]`
- Reference-adapter export: `CoaxialLumpedPort` maps to the reference terminal-modeler coaxial lumped port through an adapter helper, and a coaxial-port scene exports its radial current source, voltage/current monitors and annular load resistors; `CoaxialLumpedResistor` maps to the reference coaxial resistor; the driven `WavePort`'s `PECFrame` becomes the reference mode-source frame while other frames export as zero-thickness PEC boxes; baseband pulses map to the reference baseband source times; an adapter helper builds the reference low-frequency smoothing spec for named mode monitors
- Experimental multi-conductor `TerminalWavePort(name, position, size, direction, reference_plane, terminals=(TerminalRef, ...), reference=TerminalRef | (TerminalRef, ...), voltage_integrals=..., current_integrals=..., basis="terminal" | "modal", reference_impedance=..., impedance_definition="power_voltage" | "power_current" | "voltage_current", conductor_model=None | "good_conductor")` declares an N-terminal quasi-TEM wave port between named PEC / PlanarPEC / CopperFoil conductors. The aperture's terminal capacitance matrices `C` / `C0` are solved once on the device-resident potential engine and the launched modal fields are the eigenvectors of `C v = eps_eff C0 v` (`L = mu0 eps0 C0^-1`); `PortSweep` columns superpose those one-watt modal fields on the calibrated per-frequency CW WavePort machinery (modal-field drives only, no lumped multi-terminal feed). Terminal voltages and currents are recovered from the measured modal amplitudes (`V = Vm (a + b)`, `I = Im (a - b)`); a direct FDTD-field terminal-current cross-check through the sided Cu bank / selected-Cu query is deferred, and on split-trace CopperFoil terminals the mode-field current loops must enclose both trace sides; the characteristic matrix comes from the actual mode field by power-voltage (`Zc = Vm Vm^H / 2`) or power-current, and the quasi-TEM `C`/`C0` impedance and loop V/I are reported as cross-checks. Terminal channels `"<port>::T<k>"` are normalized to a scalar, per-terminal or full real symmetric positive-definite reference-impedance matrix (or `"characteristic"`); `basis="modal"` exposes one-watt modal channels `"<port>::M<m>"`, and `NetworkData.to_basis("modal")` converts a terminal network through the recorded terminal-to-modal transform. CPW, multiple reference conductors and multiple terminals require explicit voltage paths; a default straight path through the footprint overlap exists only for one signal over one reference separated along one tangential axis. Every conductor crossing the aperture must be a declared terminal or reference, and integrals are audited against the resolved conductor topology. Because the quasi-static basis grounds the whole aperture boundary, each port reports the share of terminal charge ending on the artificial (non-conductor) aperture walls and raises `TerminalWavePortApertureWarning` above 5 %; for microstrip-like lines keep the aperture top at least 7 substrate heights above the ground and at least 6.5 substrate heights beyond the outermost strip edge (on the coupled pair this moves the common-mode wall charge from 10.7 % to 4.6 % and |Scc21| at 0.35 GHz from 0.952 to 0.983, 0.987 with 10 h margins). Gates: coupled stripline even/odd Z0 converges to Cohn's exact values (Richardson < 0.5 %), coupled microstrip even/odd eps_eff / Z0 lie within 6 % / 4 % of Kirschning-Jansen at h/16 with monotone eps_eff convergence, CPW loop V/I equals the power-voltage Z to 1e-6 independent of loop distance from the conductor edges, and a coupled-microstrip thru with a coupled characteristic reference gives `|Sdd21| > 0.97` with `|Sdc| < 0.02`. Forward-only: trainable inputs, direct `PortExcitation`, lossy/magnetic cross-sections and mixing with single-mode `WavePort`s fail explicitly
- Custom 2D and composite voltage/current integrals for `TerminalWavePort`: `AxisSegment`, `PolylinePath`, `CustomVoltageIntegral`, `RectangleLoop` / `PolygonLoop` / `CircleLoop`, `CustomCurrentIntegral`, `CompositeVoltageIntegral` and `CompositeCurrentIntegral`. Voltage paths lower to deterministic node-to-node primal-edge staircases (off-grid vertices are rejected, never snapped), so the integral of a gradient field is exactly a potential difference; current loops lower to the boundary of the enclosed dual cells, so the circulation is exactly the enclosed discrete Ampere current for any loop shape (loops through a Yee node are rejected). The existing `AxisPath` / `Box` V/I lowering of LumpedPort, TerminalPort and WavePort is unchanged
- `NetworkData(reference_matrix=[F, N, N])` / `NetworkData.from_z(..., reference_matrix=...)` carry a full real symmetric positive-definite reference-impedance matrix with generalized power waves `a, b = (8 Zr)^-1/2 (V +- Zr I)`; `to_z`, `to_y`, `renormalize`, `to_basis` and `to_mixed_mode(..., z0=...)` support it and persistence round-trips it, while termination, cascade, reference-plane shifting and Touchstone export require a diagonal reference first. `NetworkData.to_basis(T, z0=..., port_names=...)` is the general power-preserving voltage-basis change (`Z' = T Z T^H`) of which `to_mixed_mode` is a special case
- Reference-adapter export: an adapter helper maps a terminal-basis `TerminalWavePort` to the reference terminal-driven wave port with one custom impedance spec per terminal, and companion helpers map custom voltage paths and current loops (and +/-1 composite currents) to the reference 2D / composite integral specs. Coupled or per-terminal references, modal-basis ports and composite voltages have no reference equivalent and are rejected

## FDFD Solver Workflow

> **Incomplete / experimental:** FDFD is not feature-complete and is not a numerical reference for FDTD. The entries below describe only its currently implemented subset; use FDTD for the validated general workflow.

- Frequency-domain simulation through `Simulation.fdfd(...)`
- FDFD supports result-time `PermittivityMonitor` and `MediumMonitor` payloads; all other scene monitors are rejected with their name and type before execution. Use `Simulation.fdtd(...)` or `FDFDConfig.observations` for other requested readouts.
- CUDA-only solver execution; `Simulation.fdfd(...)` requires `Scene(device="cuda")`
- Single-frequency or multifrequency public wrapper with `frequency=` or `frequencies=`
- Dispersive-material support via effective complex `epsilon_r(omega)` at the simulation frequency
- Isotropic conductive-material support via `sigma_e` folded into effective complex `epsilon_r(omega)`
- Axis-aligned diagonal electric anisotropy and diagonal `sigma_e_tensor` support in the Yee-grid operator
- Per-face `none` / `pml` / `pec` boundaries, including one-sided and mixed-axis PML layouts, exact staircase in-domain PEC constraints, and graded Cartesian Yee metrics
- Explicit fast-fail validation for gyromagnetic response, Kerr/nonlinear media and full off-diagonal `Tensor3x3` permittivity
- Configurable GMRES settings via `GMRES(max_iter, tol, restart, solver_type, preconditioner)`
- GPU-native preconditioners for the iterative solvers: `none`, `jacobi` (default), `ssor` (relaxation via `GMRES(ssor_omega=...)`, default 0.8), `ilu` (ILU(0); unstable on the indefinite curl-curl operator — see `benchmark/FDFD_PERFORMANCE.md`), and `ams` (experimental in-repo Hiptmair–Xu auxiliary-space preconditioner with geometric multigrid; measured non-contractive on the indefinite time-harmonic operator — see the module docstring for its scope)
- Double-precision assembly, solves and fields via `GMRES(precision="double")`; scene material sampling retains the compiler's original precision
- Supported `solver_type` values: `gmres`, `cg`, `direct`, plus in-repo GPU Krylov engines `bicgstab`, `tfqmr`, `idr` (IDR(s)), and `sqmr` (simplified QMR; exploits the complex-symmetric system)
- AMS coarse solves and IDR shadow-space QR / small dense solves use `torch.linalg` on CUDA through zero-copy DLPack views, avoiding import-order-dependent dense-solver library loading.
- The FDFD system is assembled in a symmetrized UPML formulation (exactly complex-symmetric via a diagonal similarity), so the adjoint solve reuses the forward factorization/preconditioner. Its stretched-coordinate PML follows the FDTD CPML parameterization (`fdfd/pml.py`). The conductivity is cubic and graded by physical depth, `sigma_max = -(m+1) ln R/(2 eta0 d)` with R = 1e-6 and kappa = 1, and `s = kappa + sigma/(alpha - i omega eps0)` is sampled at primal nodes for E derivatives and at half nodes for H derivatives. `BoundarySpec.strength` in (0, 100] scales `sigma_max`; 0 or a value above 100 keeps it nominal. The symmetrization scale depends on frequency and is rebuilt by `set_frequency`
- `direct` solver backed by NVIDIA cuDSS (`pip install witwin-maxwell[direct]`): factorize-once / solve-many with the factorization cached on the prepared solver and reused across source changes; requested complex64/complex128 LU plus iterative refinement
- Direct-solve reuse composes with `solver.set_frequency(...)`, which releases the stale factorization automatically
- FDFD solves clear previous fields and convergence diagnostics at entry and propagate the original linear-solve exception through `Simulation.run()`; successful solve computations are unchanged.
- `GMRES(device_memory_limit_bytes=...)` budgets device memory for cuDSS hybrid factors with host storage; `Result.release_solver()` frees solver-held direct factors and drops the solver, leaving allocator pool trimming to the caller
- Typed FDFD solver configuration through `FDFDConfig(solver=..., enable_plot=..., verbose=...)`
- Prepared execution via `Simulation.prepare()` before running
- Differentiable FDFD: scenes with trainable parameters (SceneModule parameters, `MaterialRegion` densities, trainable geometry tensors) route through an adjoint gradient bridge, so `loss.backward()` on `Result` fields propagates to material and geometry inputs. The pullback uses the same compiled electric Yee-edge materials as the forward matrix, retaining complex permittivity cotangents for geometry that moves conductive material. The adjoint solve reuses the scaled symmetric system and its cached direct factorization.
- System-matrix caching on the prepared solver: repeated `solve()` calls reuse the assembled matrix across source changes
- Frequency switching on the prepared solver via `solver.set_frequency(...)`, reusing compiled material components when all materials are frequency-independent
- Unified `Result` output containing `Ex`, `Ey`, and `Ez`
- Solver stats including convergence flag, solver info, residual, and solver configuration

## FDTD Solver Workflow

- Benchmark display-field and flux runtime-convergence flags share one doubled-duration FDTD rerun. Relative field L2 or maximum relative flux changes above 1% mark the corresponding metrics `unconverged` and exclude them from pass/fail counts and summaries. `WITWIN_BENCHMARK_SKIP_CONVERGENCE=1` skips both checks.
- CUDA Graph preparation restores nonzero electric/magnetic ADE, Mur, wire and other carried state on both successful capture and warmup failure, using live device storage without expanding slab CPML buffers. Resume format v3 derives its complete physical-state layout from the checkpoint schema, including network recurrence, bidirectional delay, wire and real/imaginary Mur histories, and preserves nonlinear envelope diagnostics; incomplete older formats are rejected explicitly.
- Mur boundaries update both real and imaginary tangential fields, including mixed Bloch/Mur domains. Adjoint checkpoints and replay carry each face's histories, and the reverse applies the exact ordered discrete transpose, including shared corners, before the native Maxwell reverse step.
- One FDTD time plan includes every field source, modal/terminal drive and circuit waveform. Source permutations preserve automatic duration and sampling; pure CW apodized sampling uses `transient_cycles` and the slowest requested/source period, followed by `steady_cycles`. Pulses and mixed CW/pulse drives record their full response from step zero. Explicit `window="none"` retains full-response recording including startup. Continuing or unknown-support drives disable decay shutoff, and future pulses delay its earliest eligible step. Callable waveforms require explicit `time_steps`; metadata reports short runs, source completion, sampling and support. Source normalization requires a common incident waveform across all field/port drives; ambiguous circuit-driven normalization is rejected.
- Raw `FieldTimeMonitor` and `FluxTimeMonitor` records are returned identically by fixed and trainable FDTD runs, including time-only runs. These recording buffers are detached and carry `differentiable=False`; adjoint objective gradients use the supported spectral monitor outputs.
- Full-tensor stability bounds use a positive-definiteness check and the inverse matrix norm with linear preparation storage. Nonlinear envelope diagnostics inspect occupied chi2 edges; linear source cells cannot trigger that nonlinear bound. Resume identity includes material laws, face configuration, network dynamics and wire coefficients, while excluding nonlinear coefficient work buffers that are rebuilt every step.

- FDTD preparation selects the final time step from high-frequency Yee material bounds, declared density ranges, modulation minima, and strength-dependent electric/magnetic ADE response rates, with a 0.9 stability margin and separate material-response resolution. `TimeConfig(dt=...)` pins a validated step for optimization and reproducibility; automatic step selection is numerical configuration and field gradients hold dt fixed. A trainable `Simulation` freezes its first prepared step across parameter updates and rejects an update that makes it unsafe; comparisons across separate Simulation objects should explicitly share `TimeConfig.dt`. Material, boundary, source and port coefficients bind after the joint wire constraint, and distributed shards use the same globally validated step. Solver statistics expose the bounds and limiting factor. For chi2 or negative Kerr increments, an automatically bounded field envelope (or explicit `TimeConfig(max_electric_field=...)` per-component bound) limits the negative permittivity increment; device-resident peak tracking rejects runs that exceed it. Gain media retain their explicit opt-in and are not classified as passive.

- Mixed Bloch/CPML differentiable forward stepping uses independent real and imaginary magnetic absorption memory. Scalar mode operators, material potentials, and sparse eigenvector seeds use the same row-major aperture layout for rectangular grids and unequal transverse spacings.

- Time-domain simulation through `Simulation.fdtd(...)`
- CUDA-only solver execution; `Simulation.fdtd(...)` requires `Scene(device="cuda")`
- Compiled native CUDA kernels are the only FDTD runtime backend; the Torch reference path has been retired from the runtime and relocated to the CUDA parity tests
- Engineering-preview multi-GPU joint solves through public `FDTDParallelConfig` and `Simulation.fdtd(..., parallel=...)`: Python remains the control plane, and `DistributedFDTD` owns an independent distributed coordinator/time loop for x-slab ownership, halos, streams, and events. The runtime is split into a rank-local `ShardEngine` (deterministic local-scene build from the partition plan, local solver, per-step phases, owned-energy scalar, local monitor/DFT payloads) and a coordinator that expresses every cross-rank operation through transport primitives (`exchange_electric`/`exchange_magnetic`, `reduce_owned_energy`, `gather_component_slabs`, `gather_monitor_payloads`, `gather_stats`), so the same coordinator drives both the in-process CUDA P2P transport and a one-process-per-GPU NCCL transport: the per-step time loop is branch-free over the transport primitives, while construction, validation, and result-gather scope do branch on transport kind (rank-local engine build, a narrower NCCL capability envelope, and sized point-to-point vs. peer-copy gather). Single- and multi-GPU execution share the same native CUDA numerical core; the six bounded standard real-field operations and legacy full-domain wrappers invoke the same implementations with different x ranges
- Monitor-first multi-GPU result semantics: `gather_fields=False` returns supported point/time and assembled plane/flux/mode payloads on `result_device` without a global field, while `gather_fields=True` preflights and gathers global electric last-step or DFT fields. y/z-normal `PlaneMonitor`/`FinitePlaneMonitor`/`FluxMonitor`/`ModeMonitor` payloads are tiled across owned x intervals; x-normal planes have one owner, with an explicit guard for ordinary `Ex` planes exactly on an internal split. Gathered `Result.save/load` and manifest-based `save_sharded/load_sharded` provide eager or lazy/gathered persistence without restoring live transport state
- Experimental multi-GPU FDTD joint-solve adjoint for a trainable `Box` `MaterialRegion` density on the pure real standard (open/PEC) path: a trainable-density `Simulation.fdtd(..., parallel=...)` scene routes through a distributed gradient bridge that captures per-shard forward checkpoints, runs one transposed reverse step per forward step (Phase 1 on every shard, a transposed magnetic halo, Phase 2, a transposed electric halo, Phase 3, then the per-shard source-term eps gradient), and gathers the per-shard `grad_eps` owned slices into a single global tensor so the existing single-GPU material pullback runs once on the logical scene. `loss.backward()` on a point-monitor-spectrum or full-field-DFT objective propagates to the density. Two-GPU acceptance covers 1-vs-2-GPU objective and gradient parity (point-monitor-spectrum and full-field-DFT objectives), central finite differences on density texels on the x-split and interior to each shard with the source and objective on the interface node, a checkpoint-capture stream-ordering contract, and bitwise-reproducible gathered `grad_eps`. Trainable geometry/material-perturbation/circuit/RF-port parameters, the legacy graded-sigma absorbers (`"pml"`/`"absorber"`), dispersive/conductive/nonlinear/anisotropic/modulated media, field shutoff, multi-source normalization, non-Box density regions, and tiled plane/flux/mode-seeded objectives are rejected at prepare before any distributed allocation. (The CPML/stable-PML absorbing update is now a supported distributed-adjoint capability — see the Track E3 distributed CPML-trainable adjoint entry below — which supersedes the earlier envelope that rejected all absorbing boundaries)
- Single-node one-process-per-GPU NCCL halo transport primitive (`fdtd/distributed/nccl_transport.py`, `transport="nccl"` shape): a rank-local transport launched by `torchrun` that exchanges contiguous Yee x-plane halos with its chain neighbours through `torch.distributed.batch_isend_irecv` (forward electric/magnetic and their transposed reverse-halo adjoint accumulations into preallocated staging with deterministic ordered `add_`), reduces a rank-local scalar with `all_reduce`, verifies cross-rank device homogeneity, and tears the process group down deterministically. Preflight fails fast on a missing/`torchrun`-mismatched launcher environment, a mismatched world size, non-Linux platforms, and an adopted process group whose world size, rank, or backend does not match the rank's expectation (rather than binding to a mismatched group; backend validation accepts a composite `cuda:nccl` device-backend spec and fails closed otherwise). A two-rank `torchrun` worker asserts bitwise halo round-trips, adjoint accumulation, ghost-zeroing, endpoint-ghost negative invariants, the matching-group adopt path, the shared-group teardown/vanished-group guard, and scalar all-reduce. The transport also exposes the engine-based coordinator primitives (`exchange_electric`/`exchange_magnetic` over the local engine's contiguous Yee planes on its compute stream, `reduce_owned_energy` via `all_reduce`, and a sized point-to-point `gather_component_slabs` that stitches each rank's owned x-slab onto rank 0 using the deterministic partition layouts). An end-to-end one-process-per-GPU `transport="nccl"` forward solve is now qualified on two ranks: each rank builds its own `ShardEngine`, the coordinator runs the serialized time loop over the NCCL transport, and rank 0 gathers the global full-field DFT (Ex/Ey/Ez) and matches an independent single-GPU reference at the same tolerances as the in-process CUDA P2P leg (rtol 5e-5 / atol 5e-6), verified by a two-rank `torchrun` conformance worker. A rank-death failure-matrix test confirms a dead peer surfaces as a bounded nonzero exit (launcher failure propagation / `ProcessGroupNCCL` watchdog timeout bounded by `FDTDParallelConfig.timeout_s`) rather than a hang.
